# TheBoysLauncher v4 Rewrite Handoff

## Current Release Status

As of 2026-06-29, v4 has been promoted to `main` and tagged as `v4.0.0`.
The old v3 `main` is preserved at `backup/main-v3-2026-06-29`.

Latest continuation notes:

- Final integration pass reconciled the completed scoped worktrees into the main repo: packaged exe smoke/build (`0a27`), vanilla launch core (`ba31`), accounts/auth (`2b74`), Discover/install/import (`e7e6`), hosted friends/social backend (`e004`), and release channels/updater (`0df8`). The integration keeps the Clean Dark Glassless UI bundle already present in the main checkout, merges the unique auth callback/session hardening, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` package helpers, disposable Postgres backend test helper, release-channel retry idempotency, and the focused E2E coverage added by the scoped passes.
- Packaged Windows exe smoke was verified in the scoped pass with Rust build output on T: because C: was full. `scripts/stage-social-backend.mjs`, `scripts/verify-package-resources.mjs`, and `scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs` now honor `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` while keeping repo-local `target` as the default. The unsigned local Tauri build produced exe/MSI/NSIS artifacts and stopped at the expected `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` boundary; packaged exe smoke passed against the generated release exe, including hosted-default backend behavior, Windows GUI subsystem/metadata, clean logs/stdout/stderr, no leaked processes, and navigation through Home, Library, Discover, Friends, Activity, and Settings.
- Vanilla launch core was re-proved through current launcher paths without resuming the broad all-version sweep. Focused Rust slices covered launch plans, repair, auxiliary download plans, delete, and Java selection; Playwright covered profile creation/setup/launch, fallback version lists, concurrent launch/update activity, delete from the details menu, process relaunch, and stopped-process presence. The representative live vanilla sample completed successfully for current release, current snapshot, old beta, and old alpha.
- Accounts/auth production readiness now keeps Microsoft desktop sign-in as PKCE/public-client OAuth with no `client_secret`, binds the local callback on IPv4 and IPv6 loopback while using `http://localhost:53682/`, adds security headers to callback pages, and blocks packaged-mode authenticated launch from redacted or renderer-supplied sessions so native launch uses the selected saved account. Scoped Rust and Playwright verification covered Microsoft callback, stored-session refresh/recovery, multiple account add/list/switch/remove, token redaction, and selected-account launch paths; real Microsoft login remains a manual smoke item.
- Discover/install/import is green without additional code changes. Current native routes cover Modrinth, CurseForge, ATLauncher, FTB, FTB Legacy, FTB Private, and Technic search/install links; focused Rust and Playwright covered provider search/install, archive install refresh, pasted provider links, import scan/review/submit state, unsupported-format messaging, packwiz compatibility, delete cleanup, and Prism/MultiMC/Minecraft/GDLauncher/ATLauncher/FTB-app import planners. Live direct compiled-test-binary smokes resolved live Modrinth, CurseForge export, and public Modrinth `.mrpack` paths under T: smoke roots.
- Hosted friends/social backend installed-client behavior is verified. Release builds default to `https://launcher.dylan.lol`, local service start remains explicit local/loopback mode only, and hosted/disabled modes hide local controls. The Home presence Playwright fixtures now exercise native stored-Minecraft-session exchange instead of preview `/dev/sessions`; websocket presence, failed-presence recovery, join paths, hosted friends search/requests, and local presence publishing were covered. `scripts/test-social-backend-postgres.mjs` creates a disposable database when `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is unset, avoiding migration-ledger drift.
- Dev/stable release-channel automation is retry-safe. `scripts/resolve-v4-release-version.mjs` reuses stable/dev version tags already pointing at HEAD, `.github/workflows/v4-release-channels.yml` accepts same-tag/current-SHA retries while rejecting same-name tags on other commits, automatic dev pushes cancel superseded runs, and stable/manual releases stay non-canceling. Preflight now requires resolver/configurator scripts, and the release-channel static suite covers idempotent version resolution, retry-safe tag publishing, dev cancellation behavior, package-resource helpers, and packaged-smoke command shapes.
- Release workflow path filters now cover the updater cleanup and release preflight plumbing. `v4-release-channels.yml` plus both `pull_request` and `push` filters in `v4-foundation.yml` now include `scripts/clean-tauri-updater-artifacts.mjs` and `scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs`, so edits to stale-updater cleanup or release-prep checks trigger the relevant CI/release verification instead of slipping through path filters. `scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs` now also requires its own script to exist on the release branch, and `scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs` statically guards the workflow path filters plus the preflight self-requirement. Verified with `node --check scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- Release workflow ordering is now statically guarded so packaged exe smoke remains between signed bundle build and bundle verification. `scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs` now checks both `v4-release-channels.yml` and the `package-windows` job in `v4-foundation.yml` for the sequence `Build signed Tauri bundles` -> `Smoke packaged executable` / `npm run smoke:packaged-exe` -> `Verify Windows bundles`, preventing future release edits from verifying/publishing bundles without first launching the actual packaged exe. Verified with `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- V4 release preflight now verifies the full `tauri:build` release packaging command chain instead of only checking that the script exists. `scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs` requires the command to run Tauri security verification, release social-backend build/staging, package-resource verification, stale updater-artifact cleanup, `tauri build`, and updater manifest generation in order; it also requires the helper files/scripts for staging, package-resource verification, cleanup, manifest generation, bundle verification, and packaged smoke to exist on the release branch. The release-channel suite statically guards the required command/file tokens and its post-promotion fixture now mirrors the real release packaging chain. Verified with `node --check scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- V4 release preflight now verifies the exact packaged-exe smoke command shapes, not just that the scripts exist. `scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs` requires hosted-default smoke to remain `node scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, local diagnostics to pass `--backend local --root target/packaged-exe-smoke/local-mode`, and off diagnostics to pass `--backend off --root target/packaged-exe-smoke/off-mode`, so release prep fails if a diagnostic entrypoint silently points at the wrong backend mode or root. The release-channel suite statically guards those exact command strings and the post-promotion fixture still exercises the preflight. Verified with `node --check scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- V4 release preflight now checks that all packaged-exe smoke entrypoints exist, not just the hosted-default smoke. `scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs` now requires `smoke:packaged-exe`, `smoke:packaged-exe:local`, and `smoke:packaged-exe:off`, giving release prep a cheap guard that local/off diagnostic coverage was not dropped without adding more full packaged-exe launches to the GitHub release job. The release-channel suite has a static preflight guard plus an updated post-promotion fixture containing both diagnostic scripts. Verified with `node --check scripts/preflight-v4-release.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe:off`.
- Packaged-exe game-process leak detection is now ownership-filtered instead of treating every new Java/cmd/ping process on the workstation as a launcher leak. Windows process snapshots include `ParentProcessId`; the post-close cleanup check only fails game-side candidates when their command line references the isolated smoke root or their parent chain descends from the packaged launcher process. This preserves the previous leak guard for native launch/process-lifecycle probes while avoiding false failures from unrelated developer/CI activity during the smoke. Static release-channel guards require `ParentProcessId`, `isSmokeOwnedGameProcess`, `processDescendsFromPid`, and `processCommandReferencesPath`. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe:local`.
- Packaged-exe smoke now owns game-side process cleanup in addition to launcher/friends-service cleanup. The smoke snapshots `theboyslauncher.exe`, `social-backend.exe`, `java.exe`, `javaw.exe`, `cmd.exe`, and `ping.exe` before/after the run, then fails with `Packaged exe smoke left game process(es) running after close` if the native launch/process-lifecycle probes leave a new Java, command shell, or ping process behind. Passing output now reports `Process cleanup: no new launcher, local friends-service, or game processes`; release-channel static guards require the monitored process lists and cleanup failure/report strings. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe:local`.
- Packaged-exe smoke now waits for the release process `close` event, not only the earlier `exit` event, before running full-run stdout/stderr and launcher-log validation. The smoke marks `theboysSmokeClosed` from the child `close` event and fails with `Packaged exe did not close cleanly; stdout/stderr may still be pending` if the process cannot close after a window shutdown/kill attempt, so shutdown-time output is not validated before pipes are drained. The release-channel static suite now guards the close-event marker and failure message while preserving the existing requirement that full-run process/log scans stay after `await waitForExit(child, 5000)`. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- Packaged-exe smoke now scans launcher log files twice: once after bootstrap and again after closing the release exe and waiting for process exit. This pairs with the full-run stdout/stderr check and catches shutdown-time log errors that were written after the first startup scan. The smoke helper now distinguishes `Packaged startup wrote severe log output` from `Packaged full run wrote severe log output`, and passing output includes `Full-run launcher logs: clean (...)`; the release-channel static suite requires the full-run log scan to stay after `await waitForExit(child, 5000)`. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- Packaged-exe smoke now validates captured stdout/stderr twice: once before the success summary for startup noise and again after closing the release exe and waiting for process exit. This closes the previous blind spot where shutdown-time panic/error output could arrive after the initial check. Passing smoke output now reports both `Severe process output: none (...)` and `Full-run process output: clean (...)`; the release-channel static suite verifies the full-run check stays after `await waitForExit(child, 5000)`. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- Packaged-exe smoke now captures the release process stdout/stderr instead of discarding it, and fails if the GUI exe emits panic, unhandled rejection, fatal, critical, error, or more than 64 KB of console output during startup. The smoke report now includes `Severe process output: none (...)`, complementing the existing launcher log-file scan and giving signed-release CI a direct guard against console-noise regressions from the packaged executable. Static release-channel guards require piped stdio plus `validateNoSevereProcessOutput`. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, `npm run tauri:build` through unsigned bundle generation (expected local `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` failure), and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`, which passed with zero stdout/stderr lines and clean process cleanup.
- Activity > Games process diagnostics now consistently use game-specific disclosure labels in the recovered frontend bundle. The process details panel has an accessible `${profile} game details` label and the visible summary says `View game details`, matching the existing Playwright contract and keeping launch diagnostics framed as game details instead of generic internals. Added a release-channel static guard for the recovered bundle tokens. Verified with `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, focused Activity > Games Playwright coverage for long Java paths and stopped/failed process rows, `npm run test:release-channels`, and `npm run build`.
- Tauri build now removes stale updater manifests and signature sidecars before every bundle build, preventing old `.sig` files or `latest*.json` manifests from surviving a later unsigned local build and masquerading as current release evidence. Added `scripts/clean-tauri-updater-artifacts.mjs`, wired `npm run clean:tauri-updater-artifacts` into `npm run tauri:build` before `tauri build`, and added release-channel static guards that require cleanup before packaging and manifest regeneration afterward. Running the cleanup against the current bundle directory removed stale updater artifacts; after the unsigned local build, no `.sig` or `latest*.json` sidecars remained, so `npm run verify:tauri-bundles` now fails clearly with `Tauri updater manifest is missing...` until a signed CI/local build creates fresh signatures and a manifest. - Disabled friends-service mode now renders cleanly in Settings instead of looking like a broken local service. The recovered frontend bundle now maps `endpointKind: "disabled"` to `Off` for both Connection and Mode, keeps only the `Check friends` action visible, and hides `Start local service` / `Stop local service` in the same way hosted production mode does. - Native Tauri launch commands now perform automatic managed-Java recovery before surfacing a launch-planning failure. `start_launch_process`, authenticated launch, stored-session launch, and server-join launch all route initial launch-plan creation through a shared native helper; when the core error explicitly asks for a managed Java runtime, the app chooses the lowest compatible recommended runtime for the profile's Minecraft version, downloads it with normal Activity events, installs it, verifies discovery, and retries launch planning. Java 8 remains exact-match for legacy profiles, newer versions use the lowest compatible recommendation, and configured Advanced Java override errors are not treated as managed-Java recovery. Verified with `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher java -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher missing_launch_artifact_errors_trigger_automatic_repair -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, `npm run build`, `npm run tauri:build` through unsigned bundle generation (expected local `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` failure), and fresh `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`.
- Updater release artifact selection is now unambiguous. `scripts/generate-tauri-updater-manifest.mjs` rejects multiple NSIS setup installers matching the configured `productName`/`version` instead of choosing the last sorted match, and `scripts/verify-tauri-bundles.mjs` rejects multiple matching MSI or NSIS setup artifacts instead of verifying an arbitrary first match. The release-channel suite now has negative fixtures for ambiguous setup generation and ambiguous bundle verification, so dev/stable publishing cannot quietly produce or validate updater metadata against the wrong same-version Windows artifact. Verified with `node --check scripts/generate-tauri-updater-manifest.mjs`, `node --check scripts/verify-tauri-bundles.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`, and the expected local unsigned `npm run verify:tauri-bundles` failure because the current setup exe is newer than the stale `.sig` and must be rebuilt with `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`.
- Packaged-exe smoke interruption cleanup is now explicit. The smoke harness tracks the active packaged launcher PID after spawn, handles `SIGINT` / `SIGTERM` by closing the active packaged process before releasing the smoke lock, and uses `taskkill.exe /T /F` as the forced fallback after a graceful `CloseMainWindow` attempt so a child `social-backend.exe` does not survive cancellation. Normal close paths reuse the same synchronous close helper. The release-channel script suite statically guards the active PID tracking, signal handlers, taskkill tree cleanup, and interruption message. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, serial `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`, and serial `npm run smoke:packaged-exe:local`.
- Packaged-exe smoke now serializes runs with an active-run lock under `target/packaged-exe-smoke/.active-smoke.lock` before launching the release exe. This prevents parallel hosted/local/off smokes from seeing each other's `theboyslauncher.exe` / `social-backend.exe` processes and reporting misleading cleanup failures; stale locks are removed only when the recorded owner PID is no longer alive. The release-channel script suite statically guards the lock, lock-owner error, stale-owner check, and exit cleanup. Verified with `node --check scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs`, `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, an intentional active-lock fixture that failed before launch with `Another packaged exe smoke is already running`, serial `npm run smoke:packaged-exe`, and serial `npm run smoke:packaged-exe:local`.
- Tauri security verification now guards the bundled friends-service process against console-window regressions, not just the main launcher GUI subsystem. `scripts/verify-tauri-security-config.mjs` requires `backend_start_command` to call `hide_backend_console_window(&mut command)` and requires the Windows helper to use `std::os::windows::process::CommandExt` with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` / `command.creation_flags(CREATE_NO_WINDOW)`, so packaged local/off diagnostics cannot regress into opening an extra console window. `scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs` now statically guards that verifier coverage. - Packaged-exe smoke now refuses to validate stale default release binaries. `scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs` checks that `frontend/dist` is newer than key frontend sources, that `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe` is newer than both `frontend/dist` and the native Tauri/launcher-core/shared Rust sources, and that the adjacent default `target/release/social-backend.exe` is newer than social-backend/shared Rust sources before comparing bundled resource bytes; custom `--exe` paths remain flexible. This caught the existing stale release exe after `npm run build` with `Default packaged exe is older than frontend/dist`, then a fresh `npm run tauri:build` rebuilt the exe and installers before stopping at the expected local signing-key requirement (`TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` unset). The rebuilt exe passed hosted-default, local, and off packaged smokes with `Frontend dist freshness: current`, `Friends-service release build freshness: current`, Windows GUI subsystem, metadata, bundled/adjacent social backend match, clean first-run state, no severe logs, and no leaked processes; hosted-default still avoided local backend state, while local/off proved the packaged diagnostic service can start, persist test state, and stop. - Activity's game-status tab now shows the player-facing `Games` label instead of visibly exposing `Processes`. During this slice, `frontend/src/main.tsx` was found corrupted to a single test assertion line; because the dirty worktree contained a large uncommitted UI rewrite, the renderer was recovered from the last-known-good built Vite bundle into `frontend/src/recovered-main-bundle.js` and loaded through a tiny `main.tsx` entrypoint rather than resetting to `HEAD`. The recovered bundle was patched so the Activity view switcher reads `Overview`, `Downloads`, `Games`, and `History`; the adjacent process-log regression now exercises the rendered Activity game-row log action. Verified with in-app Browser QA against `http://127.0.0.1:1420/`, `npm exec playwright -- test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --project desktop-chromium --grep "activity screen loads|activity process actions|native process action failures|activity process log export" --workers=1`, and `npm run build`.
- The default vanilla compatibility npm smoke is usable again and intentionally scoped to a representative sample instead of the broad all-version campaign. `smoke:live:vanilla:compat` now runs `run-vanilla-compat-campaign.mjs --sample --shared-cache --quiet`, which currently selects one release, one snapshot, one old beta, and one old alpha; the release-channel script suite statically guards that it keeps `--sample` and does not default to `--all`. A dry run selected `26.2`, `26.3-snapshot-2`, `b1.8.1`, and `a1.2.6`; the full sample smoke passed all four and `npm run smoke:live:vanilla:summary` now reports the `sample` campaign as 4/4 passed. - Packaged-exe smoke now has first-class local/off diagnostic coverage for the bundled friends service. `package.json` exposes `smoke:packaged-exe:local` and `smoke:packaged-exe:off`; both modes strip ambient backend overrides, assign isolated loopback binds (`127.0.0.1:4075` / `127.0.0.1:4076`), pass an explicit `THEBOYS_BACKEND_STATE_PATH` under the smoke root plus a direct test session secret, verify the packaged app auto-starts `social-backend.exe`, create a local dev session, write an `online` presence probe, assert `data/social-backend-state.json` persisted under the isolated root, and then fail if the launcher or backend process survives window close. Minecraft still works.`, avoids `/dev/sessions` fallback, and hides `backend` / HTTP status text from the normal Activity/status path; missing/expired Minecraft sign-in still prompts sign-in as before. - Automatic Java recovery now uses the launching profile's Minecraft version when a missing-managed-Java failure does not include an explicit required Java major. The renderer mirrors the core generation table for fallback selection and treats legacy Java 8 as exactly Java 8, not "8 or newer", so a 1.16.5 profile auto-installs the Java 8 recommendation instead of trying Java 17/21 and failing again. Manual `Prepare Java` recovery buttons also pass the affected profile id into recommendation selection. - Packaged-exe smoke now scans the isolated first-run `logs/` tree for severe startup failures after bootstrap. `scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs` rejects panic, unhandled rejection, fatal/critical/error log lines before a release exe can pass just because it opened a Tauri window and wrote default JSON; passing output now reports `Severe startup logs: none (...)`. - Stored Minecraft account listing now recovers from stale active-account metadata the same way launching already did. If `minecraft-accounts.json` names an active account id that is no longer present, `list_minecraft_accounts` falls back to the first saved account and marks it active instead of showing no selected account while launch uses one internally. This keeps packaged multi-account switching consistent after interrupted sign-in/removal or old metadata. - Packaged-exe smoke now owns the post-run process cleanup check instead of relying on a manual sweep. After closing the Tauri window, `scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs` compares Windows process snapshots from before and after the smoke and fails if the run leaves a new `theboyslauncher.exe` behind; hosted-default smoke also fails if a local `social-backend.exe` remains running. Passing smoke output now includes `Process cleanup: no new launcher or local social-backend processes`. The release-channel script suite statically guards these cleanup assertions. - Packaged-exe smoke now verifies first-run defaults are clean for normal users. This keeps automatic Java and simple first-run launch behavior as the default, with overrides remaining advanced-only. The release-channel script suite statically guards these smoke assertions. - Release packaging workflows now fail fast when they are not running in the official `dilllxd/theboyslauncher` repository. `v4-release-channels.yml` checks the repository immediately after channel resolution and before Node/Rust setup; the manual `package-windows` job in `v4-foundation.yml` checks immediately after checkout. Normal foundation verification still runs in PR/codex contexts, but expensive signed Windows packaging cannot burn minutes or produce updater metadata from a forked repository. The release-channel script suite statically guards the check, message, and ordering before setup/build. Verified with `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, and `npm run build`.
- Updater manifest generation and bundle verification now refuse forked or poisoned GitHub repository environments. `scripts/generate-tauri-updater-manifest.mjs` always publishes installer URLs for `dilllxd/theboyslauncher` and fails if `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` / `GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER` point elsewhere; `scripts/verify-tauri-bundles.mjs` applies the same official-repo gate before accepting manifests. This aligns release scripts with the packaged app's native trusted-installer validation, so CI cannot green-light updater metadata that the desktop app would later reject. The release-channel test suite now includes a forked-repository fixture for both generator and verifier. - Packaged-exe smoke now strips ambient launcher/backend env overrides before launching the release exe. `scripts/smoke-packaged-exe.mjs` clears inherited `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_DATA_DIR`, `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_CONFIG_DIR`, `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_CACHE_DIR`, `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_LOG_DIR`, `THEBOYS_BACKEND_EXE`, `THEBOYS_BACKEND_BIND`, `THEBOYS_BACKEND_STATE_PATH`, and `THEBOYS_BACKEND_SESSION_SECRET`, then sets only the isolated `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR` and requested backend mode. - Hosted social verification no longer depends on a developer-local loopback service by default. `npm run social:check` now checks the public hosted endpoint (`https://launcher.dylan.lol/health`) and required CORS origins on its own; local loopback verification is opt-in via `THEBOYS_CHECK_LOCAL_BACKEND=true` or `THEBOYS_LOCAL_BACKEND_HEALTH=...`. `docs/RELEASE_RUNBOOK.md` now documents public hosted verification separately from backend-host local loopback verification, so release/deployment checks can run cleanly on machines that are not hosting the backend. - Release-channel CI no longer builds the frontend twice before publishing Windows installers. The `v4-release-channels.yml` release job now relies on Tauri's `beforeBuildCommand` during `npm run tauri:build` for the channel-aware frontend build, instead of running a standalone `npm run build` and then immediately rebuilding through Tauri. `scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs` has a static guard that requires `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` to keep `beforeBuildCommand: npm run build`, requires the signed Tauri step to pass `VITE_THEBOYS_RELEASE_CHANNEL`, and rejects reintroducing a standalone `Build frontend` / `npm run build` step in the release-channel workflow. This trims billed release minutes while preserving the actual packaged frontend build path. Verified with `node --check scripts/test-release-channel-scripts.mjs`, `npm run test:release-channels`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`.
- Hosted friends-service startup now avoids preparing local backend state in production mode. `SocialBackendService::from_env` resolves the hosted endpoint first and, when hosted mode is active, leaves the local state path, session secret, and packaged local-service executable unset; explicit `THEBOYS_SOCIAL_BACKEND_URL=local` still prepares the local diagnostic service state/secret/executable as before. This prevents normal packaged clients from creating local friends-service secrets or wiring local backend controls just because the local service binary is bundled for fallback diagnostics. - Native update-installer handoff now enforces the same trusted asset shape as the renderer and bundle verifier. `open_external_url` accepts only official GitHub release downloads for `dilllxd/theboyslauncher` whose path is exactly `/releases/download/{dev-latest|vX.Y.Z[-...]}/...`, rejects query strings/fragments/lookalike assets, and opens only the expected Windows NSIS setup executables (`TheBoysLauncher_<version>_x64-setup.exe` for matching stable tags or `TheBoysLauncher%20Dev_<version>_x64-setup.exe` for `dev-latest`). Loose `.exe` files and MSI assets under the release are no longer enough to pass native validation, so the packaged app does not rely solely on renderer manifest checks before handing a URL to the OS. The updater manifest generator and bundle verifier now enforce the same invariant at artifact level: stable manifests must use `v<configured-version>`, and dev manifests must use `dev-latest`.
