Minecraft sessions through `/sessions/minecraft` before falling back to `/dev/sessions`, validate cached backend bearer responses through `/sessions/current` including `sessionKind` and Minecraft UUID/name claims so stale Minecraft-backed bearers are discarded when the local stored Minecraft account changes, upgrade a cached `sessionKind: dev` backend bearer to a Minecraft-backed bearer as soon as a usable stored Minecraft session exists, and refresh once on `401` while sending presence/friend request/accept/block/unblock/mute/unmute mutations to the local social backend when available, use detected import names when planning imported profiles, auto-load native events/processes when Activity opens, subscribe to native launcher event and managed-process pushes, merge returned operation-plan and managed-process lifecycle events into the Activity timeline immediately, summarize recent launcher operations with compact progress bars, operation type/subject context, and a latest-operation step detail panel with completed/active/pending step counts plus download-artifact current/started/finished/failed counts when artifact events are present, counting both downloaded and already-present artifacts as finished, split Activity into Overview/Processes/Events modes so the desktop surface no longer shows full process and raw event logs at once while Overview still keeps recent completion events visible, expose process-output refresh, stop, clear-exited, log export, and valid/expired/redacted Minecraft session controls in Activity/Settings, and keep graceful web-preview fallbacks.
- The desktop shell resolves a configured hosted social backend before local loopback fallback. `THEBOYS_SOCIAL_BACKEND_URL` can be provided at runtime or compile time as an origin such as `https://launcher.dylan.lol`; release builds default to `https://launcher.dylan.lol` when no override is baked in, while debug builds stay local unless configured. Set `THEBOYS_SOCIAL_BACKEND_URL=local` (also accepts `loopback`, `disabled`, or `off`) to force the packaged/local backend fallback for development or offline support. In hosted mode the client health-checks the hosted `/health`, reports `endpointKind=hosted`, does not auto-start a local backend, disables the Settings `Start local` action, and only allows native stored-Minecraft-token exchange against the native-resolved backend URL. In local mode the shell can start/stop either a `THEBOYS_BACKEND_EXE` override, an adjacent packaged `social-backend` binary, or a binary resolved through Tauri's resource directory; status checks reap exited managed backend children, and managed starts pass `THEBOYS_BACKEND_STATE_PATH` using the app data `social-backend-state.json` by default unless explicitly overridden. Managed local starts also pass `THEBOYS_BACKEND_SESSION_SECRET`: an explicit environment override wins, otherwise Tauri creates and reuses an app-data `social-backend-session-secret` file written atomically with private file permissions (`0600` on Unix and a protected owner/Admin/SYSTEM DACL on Windows), so packaged local social sessions no longer use the backend binary's hardcoded direct-run development fallback secret. The Tauri desktop config now has an explicit CSP instead of `null`: scripts are self-only, inline styles are allowed for React style props, image sources are limited to self/data/Tauri asset sources, and `connect-src` is limited to Tauri IPC, the concrete hosted release origin `https://launcher.dylan.lol`/`wss://launcher.dylan.lol`, and loopback HTTP/WebSocket endpoints for the dev server and local fallback backend. The bundle-facing Tauri app version is `4.0.0` so Windows MSI/NSIS package generation remains MSI-compatible, while the npm workspace can still carry the `4.0.0-alpha.0` scaffold label. `npm run tauri:dev` now verifies the Tauri security config, builds/stages/verifies a debug `social-backend` binary into `src-tauri/resources`, while `npm run tauri:build` verifies the Tauri security config and builds/stages/verifies the release backend before invoking `tauri build`, and the Tauri bundle config includes the staged backend resources. `npm run verify:tauri-security` asserts the CSP is present, the bundle version is MSI-compatible, includes required Tauri IPC/hosted/loopback directives, and excludes broad remote fragments; `npm run verify:package-resources:debug` and `npm run verify:package-resources` assert that staged backend resources exist, are non-empty, match the built backend binary size and SHA-256 digest, and are declared in `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`; `npm run verify:tauri-bundles` verifies the MSI and NSIS release bundle artifacts produced by `npm run tauri:build`.
- Launcher-core local paths can now be isolated for portable runs and live verification with `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR`, which maps to `data/`, `config/`, `cache/`, and `logs/` under that root. Exact per-directory overrides `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_DATA_DIR`, `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_CONFIG_DIR`, `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_CACHE_DIR`, and `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_LOG_DIR` take precedence over the root override, and settings/profiles/Minecraft-session files resolve through the overridden config directory. Ignored live WinterPack smoke tests still default to private temp roots when run directly, but `scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs` passes `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_LIVE_TEST_ROOT` so scripted live smokes leave inspectable artifacts under `target/live-smoke/winterpack`.
- `.github/workflows/v4-foundation.yml` is the new rewrite-specific CI gate, separate from the legacy Go/Fyne release workflows. It runs on Windows for v4 pull requests and v4-path pushes, installs Node/Rust dependencies, checks Rust formatting, runs `cargo check --workspace`, runs launcher-core and social-backend tests, builds the frontend, verifies the Tauri CSP/security config, builds/stages/verifies the debug `social-backend` Tauri resource, and runs the Tauri-minimum desktop Playwright smoke for shell/minimum-width coverage.
- Native launch commands now record launch-start operation events, and failed command-spec/process-spawn starts record failed launch operation events before returning the native command error; native install/repair commands now record their operation plan, build the related vanilla artifact download plan from the curated pack/profile game version, execute the vanilla downloader while streaming queued/planning/per-artifact/verifying/completed events into the launcher event log, derive pack installs from live packwiz metadata, persist the installed pack profile, record/execute auxiliary pack/modloader artifact events, and append top-level completed or failed install/repair events so Activity has a clear terminal status. Forge/NeoForge install and repair now run cached client installer processors after auxiliary processor jars are downloaded, stream queued/planning/verifying/completed processor events into Activity as each processor runs, and emit failed processor events before processor preparation/execution/verification errors return. Repair now uses the live remote catalog for profiles marked with installed pack metadata so packwiz-managed pack files are repaired alongside vanilla and modloader artifacts, core packwiz modloader planning derives loader/game-version from standard `pack.toml` metadata instead of stale saved profile fields, and manual profiles keep the modloader-only fallback.

The legacy app remains in the repository root as the existing Go/Fyne source files. It has not been deleted on this branch.

It should be treated as migration/reference material only while new v4 code is added alongside it.

## Important Environment Finding From Old PC

On the original PC:

- Node.js was installed: `node.exe 24.18.0`.
- `npm` was installed.
- Rust/Cargo were not installed.
- Go was not on PATH.

The next PC should be checked fresh; do not assume anything is installed.

## Required Tooling On New PC

Minimum:

- Git
- Node.js 20+
- Rust stable via rustup
- Microsoft C++ Build Tools with "Desktop development with C++" workload
- WebView2 Runtime
- npm dependencies via `npm install`

Recommended for full E2E/integration workflow:

- Playwright browsers: `npx playwright install`
- Docker Desktop for local Postgres-backed backend integration tests through `docker-compose.social.yml` on `127.0.0.1:55432`
- VS Build Tools or full Visual Studio C++ workload

All non-Docker commands above pass on the current machine. The latest desktop UI refinement calms the home/sidebar surface at the 1100x720 floor: the sidebar/status card is less visually heavy, the home hero has a compact primary-pack status strip, shell spacing is slightly tighter, and Playwright now asserts those status elements while still checking home/settings/activity/library overflow at the desktop minimum. The latest hosted-auth hardening upgrades Minecraft backend sessions to `minecraft-session:v2` tokens that sign the verified Minecraft UUID/name alongside account id and expiry, exposes those claims from both `/sessions/minecraft` and `/sessions/current`, keeps legacy `minecraft-session:v1` parsing for cached-token compatibility, and makes the frontend discard cached Minecraft-backed social bearers when their claims no longer match the local stored Minecraft session. The latest NeoForge hardening preserves top-level `install_profile.libraries` when an installer also ships `version.json`, which fixes NeoForge universal-jar dependency planning; a fixture regression test covers the merge, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs neoforgeInstaller` verifies the real NeoForge 21.1.1 installer from NeoForged Maven extracts BootstrapLauncher launch metadata plus the universal dependency, and `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs neoforgeProcessors` downloads the real NeoForge processor jars/classpath dependencies and verifies Java command specs resolve from cached `Main-Class` manifests with substituted arguments, extracted installer-data inputs, and Maven-layout generated artifact paths. The latest live modpack coverage adds a public GitHub-hosted Fabric packwiz planning smoke (`More Mod Variants`) that derives profile metadata from standard `pack.toml`, reads the live `index.toml`, confirms `.pw.toml` pack file planning, and verifies Fabric modloader metadata planning; the current Dylan catalog still only lists WinterPack, so this external Fabric smoke fills part of the non-Forge coverage gap until first-party Fabric/NeoForge catalog entries exist. The latest desktop UI refinement splits Activity into Overview, Processes, and Events modes: Overview keeps operation summaries, latest-step detail, and the five most recent events visible, while full process controls and raw event history live behind their dedicated modes; Playwright covers the mode behavior and the 1100x720 desktop minimum. The latest hosted-backend auth hardening disables `/dev/sessions` by default whenever `DATABASE_URL` is configured, while keeping local JSON/no-database dev sessions available; controlled hosted test environments can explicitly re-enable the route with `THEBOYS_BACKEND_ALLOW_DEV_SESSIONS=true`, and config/route tests cover the default lockout. The latest managed-Java hardening lets the native recommended-Java command fetch a hosted/versioned runtime manifest from `THEBOYS_JAVA_RUNTIME_MANIFEST_URL`, accepting either a bare array or `{ "runtimes": [...] }`, normalizing entries, validating HTTP(S) archive URLs and safe archive names, and falling back to the bundled Temurin recommendations when the hosted manifest is unavailable; Playwright covers native-hosted manifest rendering in Settings at the desktop minimum. The latest live lifecycle hardening adds a WinterPack delete smoke that installs real artifacts, verifies launch preflight, deletes the managed profile, and checks that `data/profiles/winterpack` and the profile catalog entry are removed while shared cache artifacts stay available. The latest focused hosted-backend hardening makes the standalone social-backend executable fail fast in Postgres/hosted mode when `DATABASE_URL` is configured without a strong explicit `THEBOYS_BACKEND_SESSION_SECRET`; the fallback development secret remains available only for direct local/dev runs without `DATABASE_URL`. The latest focused auth/backend hardening adds a Tauri-managed per-install local session signing secret for the packaged social backend, persisted as `social-backend-session-secret` under app data unless `THEBOYS_BACKEND_SESSION_SECRET` is explicitly configured, so packaged local social sessions no longer use the backend binary's hardcoded direct-run development fallback secret; that file is now written atomically with private permissions (`0600` on Unix and a protected owner/Admin/SYSTEM DACL on Windows). The latest focused distribution pass fixed the Windows bundle blocker by changing the Tauri bundle-facing app version to MSI-compatible `4.0.0`, while keeping the npm workspace alpha label, and `npm run tauri:build` now produces both `target/release/bundle/msi/TheBoysLauncher_4.0.0_x64_en-US.msi` and `target/release/bundle/nsis/TheBoysLauncher_4.0.0_x64-setup.exe`; `npm run verify:tauri-bundles` verifies those release artifacts. The latest focused desktop security hardening replaces the Tauri `csp: null` setting with a scoped CSP for self-only script execution, constrained image/font/object/frame/form/base directives, and Tauri IPC plus loopback-only HTTP/WebSocket connections for the dev server and packaged social backend; `npm run verify:tauri-security` now guards that config locally and in CI. The latest focused auth hardening redacts the Minecraft access token from all renderer-facing stored-session snapshots/responses, keeps native refresh/launch paths using the full token from local storage, and adds `exchange_stored_minecraft_session_for_backend_session` so the desktop renderer receives backend social authorization without posting a Minecraft token itself; the native helper now rejects non-loopback and non-HTTP backend health URLs so renderer-provided exchange targets cannot forward the token to arbitrary remote origins. Playwright covers the native stub path and asserts the browser `/sessions/minecraft` route is not used. The latest focused profile-lifecycle checks add a native `delete_profile` command, guarded launcher-core deletion of only the managed `data/profiles/<id>` directory, Activity events for completed/failed deletes, a Library delete button, an explicit delete confirmation prompt, and Playwright coverage for both confirmed and canceled preview delete flows. A fresh native `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` smoke was launched against isolated `target/tauri-dev-smoke/launcher-root`; it staged/verified the debug `social-backend` resource, opened the real `theboyslauncher.exe` window, and Computer Use verified the Home and Library accessibility trees plus visible Library `Delete` buttons before the dev runner was stopped. The latest focused downloader checks verify bounded retry after a transient 503, no retry for deterministic 404 responses, conservative retry-status classification, per-artifact start events streaming before writer/download work, streamed failed-artifact events before errors return, the Tauri bridge recording streamed download events into the desktop launcher event log, and the surrounding download-item writer/path behavior. The latest live smoke runner passes were `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs install --fresh`, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs repair`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch`, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs delete`, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs fabricPlan`, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs neoforgeInstaller`, and `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs neoforgeProcessors`, isolated under `target/live-smoke/winterpack` where applicable; install downloaded real WinterPack vanilla, packwiz, Forge, and mod artifacts, persisted a Forge `winterpack` profile for Minecraft `1.20.1` / pack version `2.3.7`, and passed launch preflight; repair then deleted a Forge launch dependency, restored it, and passed launch preflight again; launch now starts the real WinterPack Java process, verifies it survives startup, and stops it through the launcher process registry; delete now removes the managed WinterPack profile directory and catalog entry while keeping shared cache artifacts; Fabric planning now proves the non-Forge standard packwiz profile/index/metadata path against the live public manifest; NeoForge planning now proves the real installer metadata/dependency path against NeoForged Maven and real processor command-spec preparation. The launch smoke exposed and fixed real Forge runtime issues: nested LWJGL native jars now extract DLLs correctly, Forge installer processors survive installers with both `version.json` and `install_profile.json`, installer `data` placeholders and bracketed Maven processor inputs resolve into the cache, Forge runtime companion modules plus the universal jar are downloaded, and BootstrapLauncher launch plans exclude the vanilla client jar from the runtime classpath. The inspectable live smoke cache currently contains real downloaded/generated WinterPack artifacts under `target/live-smoke/winterpack`. The latest interactive desktop smoke launched `npm run tauri:dev` against an isolated `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR`, verified the native Tauri window at 1280x820, confirmed the packaged social backend can be started from Settings and reports `/health` reachable as a managed process, confirmed the Play action fails safely with a repairable missing-artifact preflight instead of spawning Java from an incomplete cache, and exercised real Repair downloads into `target/interactive-smoke` up to a 913 MB cache. That live Repair smoke exposed stale seeded WinterPack metadata (`fabric`/`1.21.1`) conflicting with the live standard `pack.toml` (`forge`/`1.20.1`); repair now resolves installed-pack profiles through the live catalog/packwiz metadata, preserves user launch customizations, persists reconciled metadata after successful repair, and download failures now include their underlying cause in the returned error text. The latest 1100x720 visual QA captured Home, Settings, and Activity web-preview screenshots under `target/ui-*.png` and confirmed no document/workspace horizontal overflow at the desktop minimum. Docker is still not installed/on PATH here, so the local Postgres-backed integration command has not been run in this workspace; backend startup applies embedded migrations when `DATABASE_URL` is configured, and `npm run test:backend:postgres` runs the ignored live migration/presence/account-link/friend-graph/block-mute persistence tests against `TEST_DATABASE_URL` or the Compose default URL.

## Latest Work Log

Latest live lifecycle update: `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs install --fresh`, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs repair`, `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs launch`, and `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs delete` were rerun under `target/live-smoke/winterpack`. Launch initially exposed Forge BootstrapLauncher runtime classpath defects; launcher-core now de-duplicates merged modloader/vanilla classpath entries and keeps installer-processor-only coordinates such as installertools, binarypatcher, jarsplitter, and ForgeAutoRenamingTool out of runtime launch metadata while still downloading them for processor execution. After those fixes, the real WinterPack Java process survived startup and stopped through the process registry.

Latest Fabric live coverage: `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs fabricInstall --root target/live-smoke/fabric` installs the public GitHub-hosted More Mod Variants packwiz fixture through the real vanilla, Fabric metadata, dependency, pack-file, and launch-preflight path, and `npm run smoke:live:fabric:launch` now starts the real Fabric Java process, verifies it survives startup, and stops it through the process registry. The install smoke exposed an older Fabric metadata shape where `launcherMeta.mainClass` is a string rather than a `{ client }` object; launcher-core now accepts both shapes, and `npm run smoke:live:fabric:plan` / `npm run smoke:live:fabric:install` / `npm run smoke:live:fabric:launch` are available aliases.

Latest vanilla live coverage: `npm run smoke:live:vanilla:launch` installs real Minecraft `1.21.8` artifacts into `target/live-smoke/vanilla`, bootstraps a managed Temurin Java 21 runtime when the machine only has older Java on `PATH`, starts the real vanilla Java process, verifies it survives startup, and stops it through the process registry. The smoke exposed that Adoptium archives extract as `data/runtimes/<runtime-id>/jdk-*/bin/java.exe`; launcher-core Java discovery now scans that nested managed-runtime layout, with regression coverage in `managed_java_candidate_paths_scan_launcher_runtime_layouts`.

Latest Java launch hardening: production launch-plan and modloader-processor planning now fail before Java is spawned when no discovered runtime satisfies the Minecraft generation requirement, instead of falling back to `javaw.exe`/`java` and surfacing a JVM class-version crash later. The error names the required Java major, the discovered runtimes, and points users to managed Java in Settings. The desktop shell now recognizes that Java-specific failure separately from missing artifacts, shows a compact sidebar `Java` recovery action, opens Settings, and loads recommended/detected runtimes; Playwright covers the native-stub Java recovery path.

Latest native launch-event hardening: Tauri managed-launch commands now record a failed `LaunchProfile` operation even when launch-plan construction fails before command-spec materialization, such as an incompatible/missing Java runtime. The event log records queued/planning/failed rows with the profile id/name and the Java error, so Activity can show the same failure that the sidebar recovery action surfaces. Focused launcher-core and Tauri tests cover the new planning-failure operation/event path.

Latest session-storage hardening: the Tauri `save_minecraft_session` command now rejects renderer-redacted Minecraft access tokens (`[redacted]`) so a safe session snapshot cannot be accidentally written back over the native token store. The Settings save-session path now surfaces native save errors instead of converting every failure into a web-preview mock session. Focused Tauri and Playwright tests cover the redacted-token rejection, alongside the existing renderer-safe session redaction tests.

Latest session-refresh recovery: authenticated launch, friend-join, and native Minecraft-backend-session exchange failures that indicate an expired/missing refresh token, `invalid_grant`, or otherwise unusable stored Minecraft session now clear the stale renderer session and expose a compact sidebar `Sign in` recovery action. Manual refresh shows the native refresh failure instead of falling back to a stale loaded session when a renderer session was present. Playwright covers the native-stub authenticated launch refresh-failure path and confirms the stale session disappears from Settings.

Latest interactive desktop pass: the real Tauri app was restarted against isolated `target/interactive-auth-smoke` with Microsoft client id `d10dfc60-1a42-44a8-b3af-edf4f5ee2c1f`; the stored Minecraft session restored as `Dilll`, WinterPack stayed `Ready` at pack version `2.3.7`, Play/Repair controls were visible, the native Repair action completed through the Activity event flow, and Library `Command` produced an authenticated WinterPack launch command preview without exposing the Minecraft token. The frontend now preserves native/profile-derived pack install state when refreshing pack metadata from the social backend `/packs` and `/packs/{pack_id}` routes, preventing stale backend preview data from turning an installed WinterPack back into `Install`; Playwright covers that regression. The Tauri `bootstrap_snapshot` command now falls back to local bundled bootstrap if remote catalog bootstrap fails, and the frontend surfaces bootstrap errors instead of silently dropping to web-preview state. Verified after these fixes with `npm run build`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "backend pack refresh preserves"`. Real Library delete/reinstall still needs an action-time user confirmation before clicking the destructive desktop Delete button.

Latest native social cleanup: launcher-core bootstrap no longer seeds native desktop snapshots with demo friends, and frontend `/presence` handling now treats the backend route as an authoritative snapshot rather than merging it into stale preview state. The Home party rail also filters out the current signed-in Minecraft account and the old preview account id, so stale local backend presence like `Player 0001` no longer appears as a friend for a real `Dilll` session. The Home hero eyebrow now switches to `Ready to play` when there are no playing friends. Live Tauri verification against `target/interactive-auth-smoke` showed `Dilll` signed in, WinterPack `Ready`, `0 parties`, `No parties yet`, and no fake Dylan/Player rows. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen replaces preview friends|home screen clears stale preview friends|home screen hides current account"`, `cargo test -p launcher-core snapshot_contains_local_launcher_data_without_demo_friends`, `npm run build`, `cargo check -p launcher-core`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest catalog placeholder cleanup: the bundled `config/modpacks.json` now contains only the real first-party WinterPack entry, removing the `ExamplePack (Dev Preview)` placeholder from native bootstrap and the packaged social-backend `/packs` route. Custom catalog parsing still supports multiple entries and wrapped `{ "packs": [...] }`/`{ "modpacks": [...] }` shapes, but the shipping app no longer advertises placeholder packs. Verified with `cargo test -p shared bundled_modpack_catalog_parses_legacy_seed`, `cargo test -p social-backend packs_endpoint_returns_curated_metadata`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "backend pack refresh preserves native installed pack state"`, `npm run build`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`. A fresh `npm run tauri:dev` restart against `target/interactive-auth-smoke` rebuilt and staged `social-backend.exe`; live Computer Use verification showed `Dilll` signed in, WinterPack `Ready`, `0 parties`, and Featured Packs containing WinterPack only.

Latest account-management foundation: the native session store is now backward-compatible with the existing active `minecraft-session.json` file while also maintaining `minecraft-accounts.json` with an active account id and multiple stored Minecraft sessions. Saving/signing in updates both stores, loading prefers the active account list but falls back to the legacy single-session file, clearing removes the active account and promotes the next stored account when present, and Tauri exposes `list_minecraft_accounts` plus `select_minecraft_account`. Settings now renders a compact `Minecraft accounts` list with active/stored account rows and a `Use` action for switching accounts. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core stored_minecraft_accounts -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "settings lists stored Minecraft accounts"`, `npm run build`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`. Live Tauri Settings against `target/interactive-auth-smoke` showed the real `Dilll` account listed as active after migrating from the existing session file.

Latest packaged-session boundary hardening: arbitrary renderer-created Minecraft sessions can no longer be saved to native storage by default. The Tauri `save_minecraft_session` command now keeps rejecting redacted tokens and additionally requires `THEBOYS_ALLOW_RENDERER_SESSION_SAVE=true` (or `1`) for local development session injection; packaged users must use the Microsoft sign-in/authenticate-and-save path. Settings surfaces the native rejection instead of silently creating a mock session in native mode, and Playwright covers both redacted and renderer-created save rejections.

Latest hosted-backend CORS hardening: the social-backend executable now reads `THEBOYS_BACKEND_CORS_ORIGINS` as a comma-separated explicit origin allowlist for hosted deployments while keeping the existing local Tauri/Vite defaults when unset. Config validation rejects wildcard origins and entries with paths/query strings, `main.rs` passes the configured origins into the Axum router, and focused backend tests cover config parsing plus hosted/default preflight behavior. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend backend_config -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend configured_cors_preflight -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend local_cors_preflight -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`. Docker remains unavailable on PATH, so Postgres-backed Docker integration is still pending.

Latest friend-discovery hardening: `/accounts/search` now requires a valid signed backend bearer before returning linked Minecraft account matches, so hosted friend discovery is no longer a public enumeration route. Short/invalid queries still return `400` after authorization, missing/invalid sessions return `401`, and the React friend search flow now calls the route through `fetchWithDevSession` so it attaches the cached dev/Minecraft backend bearer and falls back to preview roster data if backend auth/search is unavailable. Focused backend tests cover authenticated search, unauthenticated rejection, short-query validation, and result limiting; Playwright covers the bearer-bearing friend-search request before sending a backend friend request.

Latest interactive desktop smoke: `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` was launched against isolated `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR=target/interactive-cors-search-smoke`. The dev preflight chain verified the Tauri security config, rebuilt/staged/verified the debug `social-backend` resource, started Vite on `127.0.0.1:1420`, compiled and opened the native `TheBoysLauncher` window, and Computer Use verified the rendered Home/Friends/Settings accessibility tree. Settings `Start backend` launched the packaged managed `social-backend` and reported `Reachable` with health URL `http://127.0.0.1:4074/health`; a live Friends search for `Dyl` against that empty backend returned `Found 0 accounts` / `No accounts found for Dyl`, confirming the UI used the running backend path instead of preview fallback. The smoke runner, app window, Vite process, and managed backend were stopped afterward.

Latest account-link privacy hardening: `GET /accounts/{account_id}/minecraft` now requires the same valid signed-session account ownership guard as the write path, so linked Minecraft UUID/name details are no longer readable by unauthenticated or different-account callers. The authenticated `/accounts/search` route remains the friend-discovery surface. Focused backend tests cover owner readback, missing-session `401`, wrong-session `403`, missing-link `404` after auth, duplicate checks, and JSON/Postgres persistence call sites; verified with `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_account_link -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest presence privacy hardening: `GET /presence` now requires a valid signed backend bearer before returning the presence snapshot, matching the already-authenticated `/presence/ws` stream and `/presence/{account_id}` update path. The React home presence loader now obtains/refreshes a backend session through `fetchWithDevSession` before reading the snapshot, then reuses the cached backend session for the websocket URL; offline/auth failures still fall back to preview roster data without opening a stream. Focused backend tests cover authenticated sorted snapshots and missing-session `401`; Playwright covers authorized snapshot reads, websocket startup after an authorized snapshot, no websocket after an authorized-but-failed snapshot, local presence publishing, and cached-session refresh after backend rejection. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend presence -- --test-threads=1`, the targeted Playwright presence group, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted verification-endpoint hardening: `THEBOYS_BACKEND_MINECRAFT_PROFILE_URL` is now validated at backend startup/config parse time. The URL must be HTTP(S), must include a host, and when `DATABASE_URL` puts the backend in hosted/Postgres mode, insecure `http://` profile verification endpoints are rejected unless they target loopback test infrastructure such as `127.0.0.1`. This keeps live hosted Minecraft session exchange pointed at HTTPS verification by default while preserving local fixture servers. Focused config tests cover invalid schemes/hosts, hosted remote-HTTP rejection, and loopback allowance; verified with `cargo test -p social-backend backend_config -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted bearer-auth hardening: social-backend now parses the HTTP Authorization auth scheme case-insensitively and tolerates normal whitespace between `Bearer` and the token, while still rejecting missing schemes, non-bearer schemes, missing tokens, and multi-part bearer headers. This makes hosted `/sessions/current` and all shared guarded-route authorization helpers line up with standard bearer auth behavior without weakening token signature validation. Focused route tests cover lowercase `bearer`, extra spacing, and malformed bearer headers; verified with `cargo test -p social-backend current_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted Minecraft-session compatibility hardening: the standalone social-backend now disables claimless legacy `minecraft-session:v1` bearer tokens whenever it is started with `DATABASE_URL`, while local/dev JSON runs keep accepting them for cached-token upgrade compatibility. Hosted/Postgres guarded routes therefore require v2 Minecraft-backed bearers with signed Minecraft UUID/name claims, matching the frontend cached-session validation path. Focused backend tests cover local acceptance, hosted-style rejection, and parser-level legacy lockout; verified with `cargo test -p social-backend current_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session_tokens -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Minecraft-session exchange hardening: `/sessions/minecraft` now rejects blank, whitespace-bearing, or control-character Minecraft access tokens before profile verification, so malformed renderer/native exchange payloads cannot be forwarded to the Minecraft profile endpoint as bearer credentials. Focused backend tests also pin the existing lifetime behavior that Minecraft-backed backend bearers are capped to the backend session TTL even when the submitted Minecraft token expiry is far in the future, and the exchange integration test now uses the issued bearer for the authenticated account-link readback path. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session_exchange -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session_response -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest server quick-join hardening: launcher-core now validates profile default-server and explicit friend/server quick-join addresses before emitting Minecraft `--server` launch arguments. Normal domain, localhost, IPv4, bracketed IPv6, and simple internal host forms still pass, while URL-like input, paths, query/fragment strings, userinfo, percent escapes, whitespace/control characters, unsupported punctuation, and overlong addresses are rejected before Java is spawned. Focused tests cover accepted common host forms, rejected URL-like addresses, invalid explicit quick-join launch plans, and invalid persisted profile defaults; verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core server -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Java argfile hardening: long Java launch command lines now materialize `@argfile` files through the same atomic/private file writer used for stored Minecraft sessions, so files that include sensitive launch arguments such as `--accessToken` receive private permissions/DACLs before the launcher hands them to Java. The long-argfile regression now asserts the generated file lands under `.theboyslauncher/launch-args` and leaves no temporary atomic-write file behind. The broader process test filter also exposed two Forge processor tests that were accidentally reading the machine Java 17 runtime; both now use the existing Java 21 fixture guard so they test processor planning deterministically. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest desktop/package verification: the full Playwright desktop shell suite passes again (`npm run test:e2e`, 104 tests). The friend-join preflight recovery test now accounts for the authenticated initial presence-session setup added by the privacy hardening while still asserting a failed native join does not publish presence or issue an extra backend session. Release packaging also passes end to end: `npm run tauri:build` verifies Tauri security config, builds/stages/verifies the release `social-backend` resource, builds the React frontend, compiles the Tauri app, and produces `target/release/bundle/msi/TheBoysLauncher_4.0.0_x64_en-US.msi` plus `target/release/bundle/nsis/TheBoysLauncher_4.0.0_x64-setup.exe`; `npm run verify:tauri-bundles` verifies both bundle artifacts.

Latest packaged session-storage hardening: launcher-core now protects the persisted Minecraft access token with the same DPAPI-backed stored-secret path already used for Microsoft refresh tokens on Windows. Legacy plaintext session files still load and are validated, but newly saved Windows session JSON no longer contains the raw Minecraft access token; native launch/auth code receives the unprotected token only after reading through the launcher-core store. Focused Windows regressions cover raw-file redaction plus round-trip loading for both access and refresh tokens; verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core stored_minecraft_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Microsoft callback hardening: launcher-core now rejects duplicate OAuth callback `code`, `state`, `error`, and `error_description` parameters instead of accepting the first value, and rejects decoded authorization codes with control characters or excessive length before building a token-exchange plan. This keeps native loopback and manual-paste callback completion deterministic around the PKCE/state boundary and prevents malformed codes from entering the Microsoft token request form. Focused tests cover duplicate sensitive parameters and control-character codes; verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_callback -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Microsoft loopback-listener hardening: the Tauri packaged sign-in listener now requires the request target path to be `/auth/microsoft/callback` and ignores unrelated loopback requests such as `/favicon.ico` with a local 404 while continuing to wait for the real Microsoft callback within the same timeout. Malformed callback requests still fail fast, and valid callbacks still get the success/failure browser response after token exchange/auth completion. Focused Tauri tests cover unsupported path rejection and a noise-request-before-callback sequence; verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher microsoft_callback -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest desktop home cleanup: the Home screen now treats `1100x720` as the desktop floor rather than a responsive-browser target and calms the first view by focusing the right rail on currently playing/joinable friends, moving non-playing friends into a compact summary, and removing the duplicate active-party label from Featured Packs. Playwright now asserts the party rail copy while the existing home/settings/activity/library overflow checks continue to guard the desktop minimum. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen renders|home screen fits|dense desktop panels"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (104 passed), and a manual Playwright 1100x720 screenshot/metrics pass saved at `target/ui-home-1100x720.png`.

Latest Minecraft-backed social identity hardening: `/sessions/minecraft` now derives the backend social `accountId` from the verified Minecraft UUID instead of trusting a renderer-supplied account id. The old `accountId` request field is tolerated only when it exactly matches the Minecraft UUID, otherwise the exchange fails before profile verification. The Tauri bridge no longer forwards `accountId` in the token-bearing backend exchange payload and rejects mismatched local account keys before sending the stored Minecraft access token. The React social client now uses the active stored Minecraft UUID for authenticated presence/friend/block/mute ownership while keeping the preview id for no-session web preview flows; Playwright covers the browser exchange payload without `accountId`, native token isolation, and native social calls using a non-preview stored Minecraft UUID. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "stored session exchanges|native stored session exchange|stored session upgrades"`, and full `npm run test:e2e` (104 passed). Docker is still unavailable on PATH, so Postgres-backed Compose verification remains pending.

Latest Microsoft token-exchange plan hardening: launcher-core now treats renderer-provided `MicrosoftTokenExchangePlan` values as untrusted at the Tauri command boundary. The validator rejects oversized or control-character authorization codes, unsupported scope sets, unsupported OAuth form keys, duplicate canonical OAuth form fields, and form values that do not exactly match the planned client id, authorization code, redirect URI, PKCE verifier, grant type, and canonical scope before any Microsoft token request is sent. Focused tests cover duplicate `code` form fields, mismatched `code_verifier`, unsupported `client_secret`, mismatched top-level scopes, and malformed codes. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_token_exchange -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_callback -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "sign in action|Microsoft callback|authenticated launch refresh failure"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Postgres verification/tooling update: the local Postgres workflow now works on machines where Docker is available through WSL2 but the Docker Compose plugin is not installed. `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run postgres:down`, and `npm run postgres:logs` now go through `scripts/manage-social-postgres.mjs`, which prefers `docker compose` when available and otherwise falls back to `wsl docker run/start/stop/logs` with the same database name, credentials, port, container name, and named volume used by `docker-compose.social.yml`; `postgres:up` also waits for `pg_isready` so backend tests do not race cold container startup. The WSL2-backed Postgres integration suite now passes from the normal repo command, covering migrations, presence persistence, Minecraft account links, friend request acceptance, and block/mute persistence across backend state reloads. Verified with the full helper-managed loop `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run test:backend:postgres`, and `npm run postgres:down`, plus `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Postgres migration hardening: `apply_postgres_migrations` now creates a `theboyslauncher_schema_migrations` ledger table before applying embedded backend migrations, records each applied migration name with a SHA-256 checksum, skips already-applied migrations with matching checksums, and refuses startup if an already-applied migration's SQL changes. This turns the hosted backend migration path from "rerun idempotent SQL" into an auditable migration history while preserving compatibility with existing dev databases. Focused tests cover stable checksum generation, migration list validation, and live idempotent reapplication with a ledger row; the WSL2-backed Postgres suite still passes for migrations, presence, Minecraft account links, friend acceptance, and block/mute persistence. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend postgres_migration -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run test:backend:postgres`, `npm run postgres:down`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted dev-bearer lockout: guarded social-backend authorization now rejects `dev-session:v2` bearer tokens whenever dev sessions are disabled, not just `/dev/sessions` issuance. Hosted/Postgres mode therefore cannot keep using a manually signed dev bearer after `THEBOYS_BACKEND_ALLOW_DEV_SESSIONS` is false; Minecraft-backed `minecraft-session:v2` bearers still validate and authorize guarded routes in that mode. Focused tests cover disabled dev issuance plus rejected dev bearers, and prove hosted-style Minecraft bearers still work for `/sessions/current` and presence updates. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend dev_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend current_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend hosted_mode_accepts -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session_tokens -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run test:backend:postgres`, `npm run postgres:down`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted relationship fail-closed hardening: Postgres-backed friend summaries, friend-request block checks, and block/mute list routes now fail with `500` if the required Postgres relationship/filter refresh fails, instead of logging the error and serving stale in-memory relationship data. Local JSON/no-Postgres mode still uses the in-memory state directly. A focused regression seeds stale friend/block state behind an unavailable lazy Postgres pool and verifies `/friends/{account_id}` plus `/blocks/{account_id}` return `500` rather than stale data; existing friend/block/mute behavior and live Postgres persistence still pass. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend postgres_relationship_reads_fail_closed -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend friend_ -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend block_ -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend mute_ -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run test:backend:postgres`, `npm run postgres:down`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted Minecraft-bearer identity hardening: guarded social-backend authorization is now async and, when Postgres is configured, validates `minecraft-session:v2` bearer claims against the durable Minecraft account link before authorizing `/sessions/current`, presence, account search, account-link reads/writes, friend/block/mute routes, and the presence websocket. A signed bearer whose Minecraft UUID/name no longer matches the linked account is rejected, and account-link refresh failures fail closed with `500`; local JSON/no-Postgres mode keeps using signed bearer validation only. The live Postgres suite now includes `live_postgres_minecraft_bearer_requires_matching_account_link`, which persists a durable link, accepts a matching bearer, and rejects still-signed stale-name and stale-UUID bearers. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend current_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend hosted_mode_accepts -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend dev_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend presence_websocket -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run test:backend:postgres` (6 passed), `npm run postgres:down`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Microsoft refresh-scope hardening: launcher-core now treats stored Microsoft OAuth scope text as untrusted when building refresh-token requests. Refresh forms canonicalize reordered supported scopes back to `XboxLive.signin offline_access`, reject duplicated scope entries, and reject missing or unsupported scope sets before any token refresh request is sent. Focused tests cover canonicalization and rejection cases; verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_refresh_token_form -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_token_exchange -- --test-threads=1`, and `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_callback -- --test-threads=1`.

Latest auth-network hardening: launcher-core now routes the Microsoft authorization-code exchange, Microsoft refresh-token exchange, Xbox Live auth, XSTS auth, Minecraft Services login, entitlements, and profile fetch calls through a shared auth HTTP client with an explicit 30-second request timeout. This keeps packaged sign-in, refresh, and stored-session launch flows from hanging indefinitely on stalled auth upstreams while leaving the artifact downloader's separate retry/client behavior unchanged. A focused local slow-endpoint regression verifies the auth client produces a timeout error; verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core auth_http_client -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_refresh_token_form -- --test-threads=1`, and `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_token_exchange -- --test-threads=1`.

Latest hosted Minecraft profile verification hardening: social-backend now verifies `/sessions/minecraft` access tokens through a timeout-configured Minecraft profile HTTP client instead of a fresh unbounded client. The default verification timeout is 30 seconds, and a route-level slow-profile-server regression proves stalled profile verification returns `502 Bad Gateway` rather than hanging the hosted session exchange path. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session_exchange -- --test-threads=1` and `cargo test -p social-backend backend_config -- --test-threads=1`.

Latest hosted presence fail-closed hardening: Postgres-backed presence snapshot reads now fail closed when the required Postgres refresh fails, matching the hosted relationship/account-link behavior instead of serving stale in-memory presence. `GET /presence` returns `500` on refresh failure after authorization, and the websocket path refreshes the initial replay before accepting the upgrade so hosted clients do not receive stale presence when Postgres is unavailable. Local JSON/no-Postgres mode still serves the in-memory snapshot. Focused regressions cover both stale-cache HTTP reads and websocket handshake rejection against an unavailable lazy Postgres pool; verified with `cargo test -p social-backend presence -- --test-threads=1`.

Latest hosted presence write consistency hardening: Postgres-backed presence updates now persist to Postgres before mutating the in-memory presence cache or broadcasting an update. If the hosted durable write fails, `POST /presence/{account_id}` returns `500` without leaving stale local presence or emitting an uncommitted websocket update. A focused unavailable-Postgres regression covers the failure path, and the WSL2-backed live Postgres suite still passes migrations, presence persistence, Minecraft account links, bearer/account-link checks, friend acceptance, blocks, and mutes. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend postgres_presence_write_does_not_mutate_memory_on_persist_error -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend presence -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run postgres:up`, `npm run test:backend:postgres`, `npm run postgres:down`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest launcher happy-path pivot: the current priority is now a visibly working desktop launcher rather than deeper hosted-backend edge hardening. The real WinterPack launch smoke still starts the actual Java/Minecraft process from installed artifacts, verifies it survives startup, and stops it through the launcher process registry. The React desktop shell now follows a successful native Play or friend Join launch by refreshing managed processes and switching directly to Activity > Processes, so the user immediately sees the running Minecraft process with Stop and Log actions instead of only a generic "launch queued" status. Playwright covers that native-stub Play flow at both desktop viewports. A small in-flight social-backend friend-request ordering cleanup was also verified so the tree does not carry an untested mutation-order change. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "opens native process supervisor|activity screen loads"`, `cargo test -p social-backend friend_ -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest interactive launch lifecycle smoke: `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` was launched against the existing installed live-smoke WinterPack root via `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR=target/live-smoke/winterpack`. Computer Use verified the real native Home screen showed WinterPack as ready, clicked Play, observed the actual `Minecraft* Forge 1.20.1` Java window open, and confirmed the launcher switched to Activity > Processes with the running Java process plus Stop and Log controls. The Log action exported `200` captured process lines to `target/live-smoke/winterpack/logs/processes/...log`, and Stop terminated the process from the launcher. That exposed a product-facing labeling bug where a user-requested stop that exits Java with code `1` was shown as "Crashed"; managed process summaries now carry `stopRequested`, lifecycle events record requested stops as completed, and the Activity row labels those exits as "Stopped" while preserving real crash labels for unexpected nonzero exits. The dev runner, launcher window, and Minecraft process were stopped afterward. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process_registry_can_stop_running_processes -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core managed_process_lifecycle_event -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "user-stopped exits|opens native process supervisor"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest install/update UX alignment: the React desktop shell now calls native `plan_install_pack` before executing `install_pack`, matching the Repair flow's visible plan-then-execute behavior. Install, Update, and Reinstall actions now show their queued/ready operation events in Activity immediately, then refresh the bootstrap snapshot and launcher events after the native install/update receipt completes. A new Playwright update-available regression proves the featured pack card exposes `Update`, invokes `plan_install_pack` before `install_pack`, refreshes to `Ready`, and surfaces the completed update event. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack|updating a pack"`, `cargo test -p launcher-core install_pack_plan -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest pack action pending-state polish: pack Install/Update/Reinstall actions now share an in-progress guard in the React desktop shell. While a native install/update is pending, the featured pack card disables the action button and relabels it as `Installing...`, `Updating...`, or `Reinstalling...`, preventing duplicate download/install operations from repeated clicks while still allowing the completed receipt to refresh the catalog status back to `Ready`. Playwright covers an update action held open by a pending native `install_pack` promise and proves forced repeated clicks do not invoke a second install. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack|updating a pack|update action is disabled"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest Fabric launch Java bootstrap: the live public Fabric packwiz install helper now reuses the managed-Java bootstrap used by the vanilla live launch smoke, so modern Fabric packs that require Java 21 no longer fail when the host PATH only has Java 17. `npm run smoke:live:fabric:launch` initially failed against `target/live-smoke/fabric` with "Minecraft requires Java 21 or newer" from the machine's Microsoft JDK 17; after the helper change it installed/discovered managed Temurin 21 under the fabric smoke root, built the Fabric launch plan, started the real Java/Minecraft process, verified startup survival, and stopped it through the process registry. The Fabric install-preflight smoke also passes against the same root. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:fabric:launch`, `npm run smoke:live:fabric:install`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest NeoForge processor readiness: the live NeoForge processor smoke now seeds/discovers the same managed Java runtime before building processor plans, so NeoForge setup no longer depends on the host PATH Java being new enough. This fixed the live smoke failure where NeoForge 1.21.1 processor command-spec preparation discovered Microsoft JDK 17 and rejected Minecraft's Java 21 requirement before reaching the real processor metadata. `package.json` now exposes `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:installer` and `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:processors` aliases alongside the vanilla/Fabric/WinterPack launch smokes. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:processors`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest NeoForge install-preflight coverage: the live NeoForge smoke surface now includes `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:install`, which installs real Minecraft 1.21.1 vanilla artifacts, bootstraps managed Java when needed, downloads/extracts the real NeoForge 21.1.1 installer metadata, downloads dependency/processor artifacts, runs the client installer processors, builds the offline BootstrapLauncher launch plan, and passes process command preflight. That exposed a launch-preflight gap for Java module paths: NeoForge uses `-p`/`--module-path` for BootstrapLauncher module-layer jars, so process preflight now validates module-path entries in addition to the regular classpath, asset index, and native directory checks. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:install`, `cargo test -p launcher-core process_command_spec_validates_module_path_entries -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest NeoForge launch coverage: the live smoke surface now includes `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:launch`, which starts the real NeoForge 21.1.1 / Minecraft 1.21.1 Java process from installed artifacts, verifies it survives startup, and stops it through the managed process registry. The first real launch exposed two launcher-core issues that preflight alone did not catch: generated NeoForge metadata plus Mojang metadata could include duplicate unclassified Maven artifacts such as `log4j-slf4j2-impl` 2.19.0 and 2.22.1, causing Java module export conflicts, and offline launches used the string `offline-player` as `--uuid`, which newer Minecraft rejects. Classpath assembly now deduplicates unclassified Maven-layout jars by group/artifact while preserving classified jars such as LWJGL natives, and offline launch UUIDs now use deterministic standard `OfflinePlayer:<name>` UUID bytes rendered in Minecraft's undashed form. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:neoforge:launch`, `cargo test -p launcher-core modloader_classpath_ -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core offline_uuid_seed_is_valid_deterministic_uuid -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest interactive desktop launch smoke: the debug Tauri shell was rebuilt with `cargo build -p theboyslauncher`, launched against `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR=target/live-smoke/winterpack` with Vite serving the dev frontend, and exercised the real Home Play button against the warmed WinterPack cache. Settings confirmed the desktop app used `target/live-smoke/winterpack` for data/log directories. The first direct-binary attempt showed why the interactive smoke should use the freshly rebuilt binary and explicit environment injection: a stale debug exe/dev-server setup could show `localhost refused to connect` or replay old launch failures. With the rebuilt binary, Play spawned a real Java process from the desktop shell, Activity switched to Processes, the process survived startup, the Log button exported `200` captured process lines to `target/live-smoke/winterpack/logs/processes/1782486758-b47266c0-8250-4e8d-b519-8e7c2765173f.log`, Stop terminated the process from the launcher, and the row labeled the requested stop as `Stopped` rather than crashed. The captured Java command line confirmed the desktop process used the intended WinterPack cache, included BootstrapLauncher on both classpath and module path, and used the deterministic offline UUID. The app/Vite/Java processes were stopped afterward. Delete was not clicked through the Windows UI in this pass because local destructive UI actions require fresh action-time confirmation; existing live delete smoke and Playwright delete/guard coverage still prove profile deletion outside this interactive stop/log pass.

Latest desktop sidebar cleanup: the launcher status card now separates connection/status text from recovery actions, caps the live status message height more tightly, and wraps recovery buttons in a compact `Launcher recovery actions` row so Java/repair/sign-in recovery controls do not crowd the sidebar at the 1100x720 desktop floor. Playwright now pins the status message label and the native Java recovery action row. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen renders|home screen fits|home repair action falls back|Java runtime recovery"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity desktop cleanup: the Activity toolbar now separates primary view tabs (`Overview`, `Processes`, `Events`) from process tools (`Clear exited`, `Live`) instead of presenting one mixed five-button strip. The grouped toolbar keeps the 1100x720 desktop floor readable while preserving existing process/event commands and active-tab behavior. Playwright now pins the `Activity views` and `Activity process tools` groups alongside the existing Activity workflow assertions. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads|dense desktop panels"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Library profile-editor cleanup: profile rows now show launch metadata as compact summary chips, separate editable fields into a labeled `profile editor` region, and group actions into launch, edit, and danger sections so Play/Command/Save/Archive/Delete are easier to scan at the 1100x720 desktop floor. The minimum-width CSS now lays those action groups horizontally when rows wrap. Playwright pins the summary chips plus launch/edit/danger groups while preserving save/archive/delete behavior. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library profile editor|library delete action|library archive action|dense desktop panels"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Library native-refresh fix: `ProfileEditor` now resynchronizes its local input state whenever the backing profile record changes, so native `update_profile` plus `bootstrap_snapshot` refreshes cannot leave stale draft values in the editor after Rust canonicalizes name/version/loader/memory fields. Playwright includes a native-stub regression where saving `WinterPack Draft` refreshes to `WinterPack Canonical` and verifies all editor controls update to the refreshed profile values. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library profile editor"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Settings toolbar cleanup: the Settings action toolbar now groups commands into labeled Runtime, Session, and Service sections, with accessible `Settings runtime actions`, `Settings session actions`, and `Settings service actions` regions. The existing Java/recommendation/backend, session, backend service, and save commands stay in place, but the dense desktop toolbar is easier to scan at the 1100x720 floor. Playwright pins each command group while the dense-panel overflow guard still passes. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "settings render memory|dense desktop panels"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Settings validation hardening: the React settings save path now mirrors launcher-core's settings invariants before invoking native `save_settings`, rejecting impossible memory ranges and blank offline usernames immediately in the desktop UI. This prevents web-preview fallback from reporting mocked success for invalid values and avoids sending known-bad settings into the Tauri command path. Playwright covers max-memory-below-minimum and blank-offline-username rejection and proves `save_settings` is not invoked for invalid drafts. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "settings save|settings render memory|dense desktop panels"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest packwiz metafile verification hardening: launcher-core now validates fetched `.pw.toml` metafile bytes against the hash recorded in the packwiz `index.toml` before parsing them or trusting their direct/Modrinth/CurseForge provider metadata. This closes the gap where direct pack files skipped writing `.pw.toml` manifests but metafile resolution still consumed unverified TOML from the network. A focused local HTTP regression proves a SHA-256 mismatch fails before provider resolution, while the surrounding packwiz index/direct/metafile tests still pass. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_metafile_fetch_rejects_index_hash_mismatch -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_metafile -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_index -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core direct_pack_file_plan -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest packwiz metadata-network hardening: launcher-core now uses a shared timeout-configured metadata HTTP client for remote catalog, packwiz `pack.toml`, packwiz `index.toml`, `.pw.toml` metafile, and Modrinth version-provider metadata requests. The large artifact downloader keeps its separate retry/client behavior, while metadata fetches now share the same bounded-hang posture as auth/profile verification. A focused slow local endpoint regression proves the metadata client times out. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core metadata_http_client -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_metafile -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_index -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest packwiz index-planning verification hardening: launcher-core now validates fetched packwiz `index.toml` bytes against the `pack.toml` `[index]` hash before parsing the index and using it to shape install/update/repair download plans. This closes the planning-time gap where the later index download item had a checksum but the network response used to decide pack files was parsed first. A local HTTP regression proves a manifest hash mismatch fails before planning files from the index. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_index -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core packwiz_metafile -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest launch-preflight classpath hardening: launcher-core now validates Java classpath wildcard entries before spawning Minecraft. Concrete classpath jars must still exist, valid wildcard entries must use the Java-supported `directory/*` shape with an existing directory, and malformed patterns such as `*.jar` now fail as repairable launch-preflight errors instead of being passed to Java. Focused regressions cover existing valid wildcard directories, missing wildcard directories, malformed wildcard entries, and aggregate missing-artifact reporting. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process_command_spec -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core process -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest launch-argument preflight hardening: launcher-core now rejects malformed artifact-related launch arguments before Java is spawned. Classpath flags (`-cp`, `-classpath`, `--class-path`) must have a real non-option value, and `--assetsDir` / `--assetIndex` must be provided as a complete pair so the asset index preflight cannot be skipped by an incomplete generated or cached launch plan. Focused regressions cover a classpath flag followed by another option, incomplete asset arguments, and the existing missing-artifact aggregate behavior. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process_command_spec -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest hosted account-link claim hardening: social-backend now binds explicit `/accounts/{account_id}/minecraft` writes to the verified claims of a Minecraft-backed bearer. Dev sessions can still exercise the local/dev account-link route, but a `minecraft-session:v2` bearer can only write the Minecraft UUID/name it actually signed; mismatched link payloads return `403` before updating durable state. This prevents a hosted client with one verified Minecraft session from retargeting its account link to another unverified Minecraft identity. Focused regressions cover mismatched and matching Minecraft-backed link writes, while existing account-link ownership/duplicate checks and Minecraft/current-session tests still pass. Verified with `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_account_link -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend minecraft_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend current_session -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `npm run build`.

Latest launcher lifecycle UX alignment: successful native Install/Update/Reinstall, Repair, and Delete now refresh native state/events and switch the desktop shell directly to Activity > Events, matching the Play/Join behavior that already switches to Activity > Processes. This keeps long-running launcher lifecycle actions visibly anchored in the event stream instead of leaving users on the card/editor they clicked. Playwright now covers install, update, pending update, repair, pending repair, preview delete/cancel, native delete event surfacing, and delete-disabled-while-running across both desktop projects. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack|updating a pack|pack update action|home repair action refreshes|home repair action shows pending|library delete action"`, `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest Home CTA launcher alignment: the Home hero primary action is now status-aware instead of always saying `Play`. Installed primary packs still launch, update-available packs show `Update` and run the native install/update plan, not-installed packs show `Install`, and repair-needed packs show `Repair` with the same pending guards as pack cards. Presence-specific Playwright checks now launch from the Library profile row when their fixture intentionally keeps the preview primary pack update-available, while the Home/update regression proves the first-screen CTA executes the update path and lands on Activity > Events. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen renders|updating a pack plans"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (118 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest install-to-launch event action: completed native install/update and repair events now expose a compact `Play` action in Activity when the affected profile exists and no active managed process owns it. This lets the first-run path continue directly from Install/Update/Repair progress to supervised Minecraft launch without forcing users to navigate back to Home or Library. The install regression now proves a not-installed pack installs, lands on Activity > Events, uses the completed install event's `Play` button, invokes the native launch path, and switches to Activity > Processes with a running Java process. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (118 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest Home secondary-action cleanup: the Home hero secondary action is now lifecycle-aware instead of always showing `Repair`. Update-available and not-installed primary packs show `Details` beside the primary `Update`/`Install` action, while installed packs keep `Repair` as the secondary maintenance action. This reduces first-screen clutter and avoids implying repair is part of the normal first-run/update path. The pack-details test now exercises the hero `Details` button, and the default Home render pins the `Update` + `Details` pair. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen renders|home screen can refresh a single pack|home details action|home repair action refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (118 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest Featured Pack launch alignment: installed Featured Pack cards now behave like the Home hero and Library profile rows: the primary card action is `Play` and launches through the native managed process path instead of calling `install_pack` as a confusing `Ready`/reinstall action. Update/not-installed cards still install/update, and repair-needed cards still repair. A new native-stub Playwright regression proves an installed pack card invokes `start_launch_process`, avoids `install_pack`, switches to Activity > Processes, and shows the running Java process. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack refreshes|updating a pack plans|installed pack card play|home repair action refreshes|friend join surfaces"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (120 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest duplicate-launch guard: Home primary pack actions, installed Featured Pack cards, and Library profile launch controls now use the existing managed-process ownership set to disable duplicate launches for profiles that are already running or stopping. Those controls relabel from `Play` to disabled `Running`, while existing Delete guards still prevent destructive profile actions during an active process. Playwright now proves an installed pack card launches once, returns to Home as `Running`, and the Library launch/danger action groups both disable their running-profile controls. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installed pack card play|library delete action is disabled"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (120 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest stop-presence cleanup: successful native Stop now best-effort publishes `{ state: "online" }` through the local social-backend presence route and merges the cleared local presence after a managed Minecraft process is stopped. This keeps the Home party panel from continuing to show the local user as playing WinterPack after the process has moved into a requested-stop state. Playwright now covers Activity > Processes Stop against a native-stub running process, verifies the authenticated backend presence payload, then returns Home and confirms the local playing pack is gone. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "user-stopped exits|stopping a native process clears"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (122 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest default-catalog launcher alignment: shared catalog normalization now orders entries marked `"default": true` before non-default entries, so the React Home hero's existing `snapshot.packs[0]` behavior follows Dylan's live catalog default instead of depending on remote JSON order as more packs are added. This keeps the first-screen Play/Install/Update path aimed at the intended official pack while preserving the standard packwiz `pack.toml` install flow. Verified the live `https://modpacks.dylan.lol/modpacks.json` response still uses the supported array shape and points WinterPack at `https://modpacks.dylan.lol/winterpack-modpack/pack.toml`. Verified locally with `cargo test -p shared modpack_catalog -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen renders"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest install-progress visibility alignment: Install/Update/Reinstall now switches the desktop shell to Activity > Events immediately after the native install plan is ready, before `install_pack` starts the long-running download/packwiz/modloader work. Successful launcher-event refreshes now merge returned native events into the current Activity stream instead of replacing optimistic plan/live-pushed events, so the first-run WinterPack path does not briefly lose its plan row if Activity refresh races native event-log persistence. The pending-update Playwright regression now proves the user lands on Activity > Events with the install plan visible while the native install promise is still unresolved, and duplicate install clicks remain guarded. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack|updating a pack|pack update action"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (122 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.
