Latest repair-progress visibility alignment: Repair now matches Install/Update by switching the desktop shell to Activity > Events as soon as the native repair plan is ready, before `repair_profile` starts long-running vanilla artifact, packwiz, and modloader repair work. The pending-repair Playwright regression now proves Activity > Events shows the repair plan while the native repair promise is still unresolved, the Home/card repair button remains disabled as `Repairing...`, and duplicate repair invocation is still guarded. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home repair action"`, the broader lifecycle slice `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack|updating a pack|pack update action|home repair action|library delete action"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (122 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest live WinterPack happy-path recheck and stop-status polish: the current launcher core still passes the real Dylan catalog/packwiz path from metadata through launch: live WinterPack catalog metadata parsed, install planning used the standard `pack.toml`, real install artifacts passed launch preflight, and the real WinterPack Java process survived startup and stopped through the process registry. The desktop Activity status message now distinguishes a requested stop from a completed stop: if native `stop_managed_process` returns a still-running `stop_requested` process, the sidebar reports `javaw.exe stop requested`; it only reports `stopped` once the process summary is exited. This keeps the process supervisor honest during real Minecraft shutdown while preserving the row-level `Stopped` label for user-requested exited processes. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:metadata`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:plan`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:install`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "user-stopped exits|stopping a native process clears|activity screen loads"`, `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest process-supervisor live-refresh alignment: successful native profile Play and friend Join launches now open Activity > Processes with the `Live` process refresh toggle enabled, so running Minecraft output/state continues to refresh immediately in the desktop process supervisor after launch instead of requiring a manual Live click. The toggle remains user-controlled and can still be turned off. Playwright now pins the native Library Play path to prove Activity > Processes opens with Live active, shows Stop/Log controls, and allows Live to be disabled. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "opens native process supervisor|installed pack card play|friend join"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (122 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest install/repair failure event surfacing: failed native Install/Update/Reinstall and Repair attempts now refresh the native launcher event log and keep the desktop shell on Activity > Events before reporting the sidebar error. Since the Rust commands already record failed operation events before returning errors, the user now sees the failed row immediately after a download, packwiz, modloader, asset, or repair failure instead of needing to manually reload Activity. Playwright covers failed native pack install and failed native repair paths, including the absence of a completed-event `Play` action on failed rows. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "failed native|installing a pack refreshes|home repair action refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (126 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest launch-supervisor immediacy polish: native profile Play and friend Join now use the `ManagedProcessSummary` returned by the launch command immediately instead of waiting only on a follow-up process-list refresh. The process list merge preserves the richer output snapshot when a later lifecycle event or poll has fewer retained lines, and a short recent-launch fallback keeps the returned process visible if the immediate native process-list refresh lags for a moment after startup. This makes the desktop Play path land on Activity > Processes with visible Java output/state right away. Playwright covers the normal Library Play supervisor path plus a lagging `list_managed_processes` regression where the returned process summary is still shown. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library play action opens native process supervisor|library play action keeps returned native process visible|installed pack card play|installing a pack refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (128 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest process-log lifecycle refresh: successful native process log export now silently refreshes the managed-process registry before restoring the export-complete status message. Since the Rust export command already reaps exited processes before writing the log, the desktop Activity row, Stop button, Library Play button, and Delete guard now reflect a clean/crashed exit immediately after exporting logs instead of waiting for Live polling or a manual refresh. Playwright covers a native-stub export where a running WinterPack process exits during export, the row changes to `Exited cleanly`, Stop disables, and Library Play/Delete are re-enabled. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads|activity process row labels|native process log export|stopping a native process clears|library delete action is disabled"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (130 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest live WinterPack happy-path verification: the current worktree was rechecked against Dylan's live catalog and the standard WinterPack packwiz `pack.toml` path after the recent desktop process-supervisor changes. Live metadata parsing, remote install planning, full artifact install-to-launch-preflight, and actual Java launch/process-registry stop all pass against `target/live-smoke/winterpack`, proving the real supported modpack path still works from catalog metadata through supervised launch. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:metadata`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:plan`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:install`, and `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch`.

Latest Activity relaunch polish: exited Activity process rows that still map to a known profile now expose a compact `Play` action. This lets the desktop happy path continue directly from a stopped/crashed/cleanly exited Minecraft process back into a supervised relaunch without navigating to Home or Library, while active running/stop-requested profile guards still prevent duplicate launches. Playwright covers an exited WinterPack process row relaunching through `start_launch_process`, returning to Activity > Processes, enabling Live refresh, and showing the new running Java output. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads|activity process row labels|native process log export|exited native process row can relaunch|stopping a native process clears|library play action opens native process supervisor"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (132 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest Activity process-action cleanup: Activity process rows now group row-level `Play`, `Stop`, and `Log` controls into a stable `process actions` cluster instead of leaving them as separate row siblings. The cluster matches the Library profile action-group pattern, keeps each compact button full-width inside a narrow right-side column, and remains wrap-safe at the 1100x720 desktop floor while the process output/details keep the flexible main column. Playwright now pins the grouped process controls for both running and exited/relaunchable rows, and the dense desktop overflow guard still passes. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "dense desktop panels|activity screen loads|exited native process row can relaunch|native process log export"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (132 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest bounded Tauri dev sanity: `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` was run against the current worktree after the Activity lifecycle/UI passes. The shell timeout stopped the long-running dev session after the debug desktop app had launched as `target/debug/theboyslauncher.exe`; the run also staged fresh debug `social-backend` resources into `src-tauri/resources`. The spawned npm/Tauri CLI/Vite/Cargo/debug-launcher processes were inspected and cleaned up afterward, leaving only the Codex runtime process. Follow-up explicit checks passed with `npm run verify:tauri-security`, `npm run verify:package-resources:debug`, and `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`.

Latest failed-launch event recovery action: failed Activity event rows for repairable `launch_profile` errors now expose a compact `Repair` action when the event has a known profile subject. This makes missing asset index/natives/classpath failures actionable directly from the event stream, not only through the sidebar recovery strip, while completed install/repair rows still keep their `Play` continuation. Playwright now drives both Library Play and friend Join launch-preflight failures from the failed event-row `Repair` button through `plan_repair_profile` and `repair_profile`. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "launch preflight|friend join surfaces native launch preflight|failed native repair|failed native pack install|installing a pack refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (132 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest failed install/repair retry actions: failed Activity event rows for native `install_pack` now expose a compact `Retry` action when the pack still exists in the catalog, and failed `repair_profile` rows expose `Repair` when the profile still exists. Both actions call the same guarded `installPack`/`repairProfile` flows used by Home and Library, so retry attempts still plan first, switch to Activity > Events, refresh native events on failure/success, and respect in-progress disable states. Playwright now proves failed install and failed repair rows do not expose `Play`, do expose their retry/repair action, and invoke `plan_install_pack`/`install_pack` or `plan_repair_profile`/`repair_profile` again from the event row. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "failed native pack install|failed native repair|launch preflight|installing a pack refreshes|home repair action refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (132 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest live repair/delete lifecycle verification: the real WinterPack repair smoke still restores a deleted Forge launch dependency and passes launch preflight, and the real delete smoke still removes the managed WinterPack profile directory/catalog entry while preserving shared cache artifacts. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:repair` and `node scripts/run-live-winterpack-smoke.mjs delete`. These live smokes share `target/live-smoke/winterpack`; when they are run concurrently, Forge installer processors can contend on cached MCP output files, so run shared-root live lifecycle smokes serially unless each command is given an isolated root.

Latest native lifecycle serialization: the Tauri command layer now manages a shared `LifecycleOperationGate` around cache/profile/runtime-mutating launcher commands (`install_pack`, `repair_profile`, `delete_profile`, profile import execution, generic download-plan execution, and managed Java runtime install). A second overlapping lifecycle operation now receives a clear "another launcher lifecycle operation is already running" error instead of letting concurrent Forge/packwiz/runtime work collide in shared launcher directories. This protects the real install/repair/delete happy path exposed by the live WinterPack smokes while leaving read-only planning commands concurrent. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher lifecycle_operation_gate -- --test-threads=1`, full `cargo test -p theboyslauncher -- --test-threads=1` (39 passed), `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest desktop lifecycle-button alignment: the React shell now derives a single lifecycle busy state from install, repair, delete, import execution, and managed Java runtime install work. Lifecycle-mutating controls disable while one of those operations is active, while normal Play/read-only details/command flows remain available where safe. Native delete failures now keep the profile visible and surface the failed event instead of falling through to the web-preview delete mock. Playwright covers pending repair button state and native delete failure recovery, and the full suite now has 134 passing checks. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "pending native repair|native failure|library delete action"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (134 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest friend-join happy-path alignment: friend Join now checks the local catalog status for the friend's pack before attempting the server launch. If the pack is not installed or has an update available, Join runs the existing guarded install/update flow first; if the pack needs repair, Join runs repair first. Only a successful readiness step continues into the native server launch, Activity > Processes, and presence publish. This makes the social join path match the product shape where missing/outdated friend packs become ready before joining rather than always failing into launch preflight recovery. Playwright now covers an update-available WinterPack friend join that calls `plan_install_pack`/`install_pack` before `start_launch_process_for_server`, lands in the process supervisor, and publishes playing presence for the joined server. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "friend join"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (136 passed), and `npm run build`.

Latest authenticated friend-join coverage: the Tauri/core launch path already accepts an optional `ServerLaunchTarget` for stored authenticated launches, and the React Join path passes the friend server payload through `start_stored_authenticated_launch_process` when a stored Minecraft session is usable. A new native-stub Playwright regression pins that behavior by asserting the authenticated Join payload includes `profileId: "winterpack"` plus the explicit `The Cabin` server address/port, then lands in Activity > Processes and publishes server presence. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "stored session|friend join"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (138 passed), and `npm run build`.

Latest interactive desktop UI/catalog sanity: a bounded `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` run against isolated `target/interactive-goal-smoke` launched the real debug `theboyslauncher.exe`, staged the packaged debug `social-backend` resource, and Computer Use verified the Home screen in the native Tauri window. That live inspection exposed two happy-path polish issues: the sidebar status card clipped the long native backend status message, and live catalog-derived WinterPack cards displayed `No server` while the social join surface used `The Cabin`. The status card now wraps native status text without internal clipping at the 1100x720 floor, covered by a Playwright desktop-minimum regression. Shared catalog summaries now support optional `defaultServer` from catalog JSON and apply a first-party WinterPack fallback of `The Cabin`, so the desktop/backend pack surfaces no longer lose the official server label when the legacy Dylan catalog omits it. The Tauri dev processes were inspected and cleaned up after the smoke. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen fits|launcher status card|dense desktop panels"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (140 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo test -p shared pack_summary -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p social-backend packs_endpoint_returns_curated_metadata -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest interactive install/launch guard: a real `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` desktop smoke was run against isolated `target/interactive-install-smoke`. The native Tauri window showed WinterPack with `The Cabin`; clicking Update switched to Activity > Events, then executing the completed plan began real vanilla artifact downloads into the isolated cache. The smoke exposed that completed install-plan event rows could still expose Play while native install/update work was in progress, allowing an early launch attempt that failed on the machine's Java 17 before install completion. React now disables completed install/repair event-row Play while any lifecycle operation is active, and the Tauri launch commands (`start_launch_process`, authenticated/stored variants, and server launch) now call the shared lifecycle gate before planning/spawning as a native backstop. The machine still needs a managed Java 21 runtime installed before a true interactive Minecraft launch can pass from this isolated root. The dev processes were cleaned up after the smoke. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "pack update action is disabled while native install is pending"`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher lifecycle_operation_gate -- --test-threads=1`, full `cargo test -p theboyslauncher -- --test-threads=1` (41 passed), full `npm run test:e2e` (140 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest Java recovery happy-path polish: the sidebar Java recovery action now opens Settings, loads detected runtimes plus recommended runtime entries, and preselects the lowest compatible Java 21+ recommendation so the user lands with the managed-runtime fields ready for `Install runtime` instead of having to manually choose the recommendation first. The managed Java install flow now switches to Activity > Events during native download/install work, merges the returned download/install operation events into the timeline, rediscovers installed runtimes on success, and keeps real native failures visible instead of falling back to the web-preview mock runtime/message. Playwright now covers the Java launch-recovery path preselecting Temurin 21 and a native-stub managed Java install that calls `build_managed_java_runtime_download_plan`, `execute_download_plan`, `execute_managed_java_runtime_install`, then `discover_java_runtimes` while surfacing Java events in Activity. The live WinterPack launch smoke still passes against `target/live-smoke/winterpack`, proving the Java-capable core launch/process-supervision path starts and stops a real Minecraft process. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "Java runtime recovery|managed Java|Java recommendations"`, `cargo test -p launcher-core managed_java -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch`, full `npm run test:e2e` (142 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest Activity install/repair continuation cleanup: completed install/repair plan-prep rows such as `Update plan is ready to execute.` and `Repair plan is ready to execute.` no longer expose a misleading Play action. The Activity timeline now shows Play/Running only for terminal successful install/update/reinstall or repair completion events (`Pack installed/updated/reinstalled successfully.` or `Profile repair completed.`), while plan rows remain progress-only. This keeps the one-click Home/Library Update or Repair flow from looking like a separate "execute the plan with Play" step. Playwright now asserts pending install/update and repair plan rows have no Play action, while terminal rows still show the correct continuation or active-process Running state. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "pack update action is disabled while native install is pending|home repair action shows pending native repair state|installing a pack refreshes|updating a pack plans|home repair action refreshes"`, full `npm run test:e2e` (142 passed), `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest interactive desktop launch/log/relaunch sanity: `npm run tauri:dev -- --no-watch` was run against isolated `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR=target/live-smoke/winterpack` with the existing live WinterPack install. Computer Use verified the native Tauri Home screen showed WinterPack `Ready`, `The Cabin`, and Play/Repair controls; clicking Play switched to Activity > Processes with Live enabled, started the real Java process (`C:/Program Files/Microsoft/jdk-17.0.19.10-hotspot/bin/java.exe`), retained live output, and exposed Stop/Log controls. Stop terminated the process through the UI, Log exported 24 retained lines into `target/live-smoke/winterpack/logs/processes/1782528365-98db138b-3dc2-48d0-aca9-78d4ff5d3976.log`, and the exited-row Play action successfully relaunched a second supervised Java process that was also stopped through the UI. The dev app, Vite/Tauri/Cargo helper processes, and Java processes were inspected and cleaned up afterward.

Latest hosted social-backend default: packaged/release desktop builds now prefer a hosted social backend instead of starting a local backend on players' PCs. Native `SocialBackendStatus` includes `endpointKind` and `endpointUrl`; release builds default to `https://launcher.dylan.lol` unless `THEBOYS_SOCIAL_BACKEND_URL` is configured at build/runtime, and `THEBOYS_SOCIAL_BACKEND_URL=local` forces the old packaged-loopback fallback for dev/offline testing. Hosted mode health-checks `/health`, disables local auto-start, disables the Settings `Start local` action, and only exchanges stored Minecraft tokens against the native-resolved backend URL. The Tauri CSP now allows only `https://launcher.dylan.lol` and `wss://launcher.dylan.lol` as remote social endpoints, so a different release host also requires updating `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` and `scripts/verify-tauri-security-config.mjs`. The local Postgres helper now binds to `127.0.0.1` by default, accepts `THEBOYS_POSTGRES_HOST`, `THEBOYS_POSTGRES_PORT`, `THEBOYS_POSTGRES_DB`, `THEBOYS_POSTGRES_USER`, and `THEBOYS_POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, prints the matching `DATABASE_URL` after `postgres:up`, and exposes `npm run postgres:url`. Verified with `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher backend -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run verify:tauri-security`, `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "backend start|hosted social backend|backend action"`, `cargo check --workspace`, `node --check scripts/manage-social-postgres.mjs`, `node --check scripts/verify-tauri-security-config.mjs`, and `npm run postgres:url`.

Latest native install/update completion alignment: the Tauri `install_pack` command now reuses the already-built install plan intent when recording its terminal event and receipt, so fresh installs report `Pack installed successfully.`, outdated installed packs report `Pack updated successfully.`, and current installed packs report `Pack reinstalled successfully.`. This keeps native Activity completion rows consistent with the Home/Library action the user clicked and with the frontend continuation rules that only expose Play on terminal install/update/reinstall completion events. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher install_pack_completion_message -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "updating a pack|pack update action|installing a pack refreshes"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`. During verification, C: only had about 72 MB free after previous builds; generated Rust build artifacts under `target/debug` and `target/release` were removed, freeing roughly 55 GB while leaving `target/live-smoke` roots intact.

Latest native duplicate-launch guard: all Tauri managed launch commands (`start_launch_process`, authenticated/stored variants, and server quick-join) now check the process registry for an already running or stop-requested process with the requested profile id before building/spawning a new launch plan. When one exists, the command returns that existing `ManagedProcessSummary` so the React shell can land on the active Activity > Processes row without starting a second Minecraft instance for the same profile. This backs up the UI-level Play/Running disable state at the native command boundary. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher active_managed_process -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library play action opens native process supervisor|installed pack card play|library delete action is disabled while a profile process is running|exited native process row can relaunch"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest friend-join running-profile alignment: Home party-rail and Friends roster Join controls now use the same active-process profile guard as Play/Delete. If the friend's pack maps to a profile that already has a running or stop-requested process, the Join button becomes a disabled `Running` control instead of implying a second server launch is available. The `joinFriend` handler also bails out to Activity > Processes when the active-process set already owns that profile, preventing stale/racy clicks from publishing misleading joined-server presence. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "friend join is disabled|friend join action|friend join updates|stored session friend join|library delete action is disabled"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest archive running-profile guard: Library Archive now follows the same active-process profile guard as Play/Delete. Running profiles show a disabled `Running` archive control, `archiveProfile` no-ops while the profile has an active process, and the Tauri `archive_profile` command checks the process registry before hiding the profile. Native archive failures now stay visible in the Library instead of falling through to the web-preview removal path. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "archive action|delete action is disabled|archive action keeps profile"`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher active_managed_process -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest stop/presence lifecycle alignment: stopping a native managed process no longer downgrades local social presence while the process is only `stop_requested`; Minecraft is still shutting down at that point. The React shell now clears playing presence only when the stopped process summary is actually `exited`, and the managed-process event stream performs the same once-per-process cleanup when the eventual exit event arrives. Playwright covers both paths: stop-requested keeps presence untouched, while an exited stop result posts `{ state: "online" }`. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "stopping a native process"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

## Fresh Foundation Plan

### Summary

Start from zero. The best route is:

```text
Tauri v2 + React/TypeScript frontend + Rust launcher core + Rust hosted backend
```

This gives a modern web-quality UI, small native desktop apps, cross-platform packaging, safe filesystem/process/download code, and a clean foundation for social presence.

### Core Decisions

- Use Tauri v2 for desktop.
- Use React + TypeScript + Vite for UI.
- Use Rust for local launcher core.
- Build first-party Minecraft launch core instead of wrapping a launcher black box.
- Use Rust backend with Postgres and WebSockets.
- Use Microsoft/Minecraft login as the primary player identity; social backend links by Minecraft UUID/name.

### Product Shape

- Home screen is social-first:
  - Play CTA;
  - friends online;
  - active pack/server;
  - featured packs;
  - install/update/repair states.
- Library supports:
  - curated packs;
  - custom vanilla profiles;
  - Fabric, Quilt, Forge, NeoForge profiles;
  - imported profiles;
  - memory, Java, resolution, JVM args, server quick-join, logs, saves.
- Server experience:
  - packs define official/default servers;
  - friends can be joined if the profile is installed;
  - missing/outdated profiles install or update first, then join.

### Implementation Phases

1. Foundation spike:
   - Tauri + React + Rust workspace;
   - local directories, settings, logging, typed events, process runner;
   - prove vanilla Microsoft-auth launch and offline launch.
2. Native Minecraft core:
   - manifest resolution;
   - asset/library/native downloads;
   - Java runtime selection/download;
   - launch args;
   - process supervision;
   - Fabric, Quilt, Forge, NeoForge.
3. Pack/profile system:
   - curated catalog;
   - packwiz;
   - custom profiles;
   - repair/reinstall/update.
4. Modern UI:
   - onboarding;
   - Microsoft login;
   - Home, Library, Pack/Profile detail, Launch Progress, Logs, Settings.
5. Social backend:
   - Minecraft UUID account linking;
   - friend search/request/accept/block;
   - WebSocket presence;
   - pack/server metadata.
6. Migration/import:
   - old TheBoysLauncher/Prism data;
   - Prism, MultiMC, official Minecraft, GDLauncher, ATLauncher;
   - copy saves/options/resourcepacks/shaderpacks/screenshots/configs without mutating source.
7. Distribution:
   - signed Tauri updater;
   - CI builds for Windows/macOS/Linux;
   - code signing/notarization;
   - diagnostics upload.

## Testing Expectations

Add E2E early and keep it running throughout.

Recommended layers:

- Rust unit tests for launcher-core models/resolvers.
- Rust integration tests for filesystem/import behavior using temp dirs.
- Backend tests using Docker/Postgres.
- Playwright tests for frontend flows.
- Tauri command tests where practical.

Initial Playwright scenarios:

- home screen renders social launcher shell;
- search filters featured packs;
- library profile Play button calls launch action;
- import screen exposes scan action;
- settings render memory/offline profile data;
- no UI overlap at desktop and small-window sizes.

Later E2E:

- first-run onboarding;
- Microsoft login mocked;
- install/update/repair mocked;
- launch progress event stream mocked;
- friend presence mocked;
- migration scan with fixture directories.

Latest profile-save running-profile guard: Library profile Save now follows the same active-process and lifecycle busy guards as Play/Archive/Delete. Running profiles keep Save disabled even after local editor changes, `updateProfile` no-ops while the owning process is active, native save failures surface their real error instead of falling through to the web-preview mutation path, and the Tauri `update_profile` command rejects saves for profiles owned by running or stop-requested managed Java processes. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "profile editor|delete action is disabled while a profile process is running"`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher active_managed_process -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Java recovery retry alignment: Java-version launch failures now remember the blocked profile while the sidebar recovery action opens Settings and preselects a recommended Java 21 runtime. After a successful managed Java runtime install, the sidebar recovery strip changes from `Java` to `Play`, letting the user retry that same profile directly into Activity > Processes instead of navigating back through Library. Playwright covers the full native-stub path from Java 17 failure to Temurin 21 install to successful process-supervised retry. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "Java runtime recovery and retry|managed Java install"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest failed-launch Java event action: failed Activity event rows for `launch_profile` Java-version errors now expose a compact `Java` action, matching the sidebar recovery flow. Clicking it opens Settings, preselects the recommended Java 21 runtime, preserves the failed profile as the post-install retry target, and the existing recovery strip then offers `Play` after the managed runtime install succeeds. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "Java runtime recovery and retry|managed Java install"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest sign-in recovery retry alignment: authenticated launch failures caused by expired/invalid stored Microsoft refresh tokens now remember the blocked profile while clearing the stale Minecraft session. If the Microsoft sign-in callback succeeds, the sidebar recovery strip changes from `Sign in` to `Play`, letting the user retry the same profile directly into Activity > Processes; if the callback times out or fails, the `Sign in` recovery remains available. Playwright covers both the existing timeout path and the successful sign-in-to-retry path. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "sign-in recovery|refresh failure offers sign-in"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest failed-launch sign-in event action: failed Activity event rows for `launch_profile` session/auth errors now expose a compact `Sign in` action. Clicking it starts the Microsoft login flow, preserves the event subject as the retry profile, and on a successful local callback the sidebar recovery strip offers `Play` for that same profile. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "sign-in recovery|refresh failure offers sign-in"`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest custom profile creation alignment: Library `New profile` now opens an inline desktop creator instead of silently creating a hardcoded vanilla profile. The creator lets users choose name, Minecraft version, loader (`vanilla`/`fabric`/`quilt`/`forge`/`neoforge`), and memory, validates the draft before calling native `create_profile`, preserves real native failures, refreshes the native bootstrap snapshot on success, and still creates a local mocked profile in web preview. This moves the custom vanilla/modloader profile path closer to the product shape needed for normal launcher use. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "creating a profile|new profile action|profile editor"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "dense desktop panels|home screen fits|settings render memory"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest vanilla live launch recheck: after the Library custom profile creator pass, `npm run smoke:live:vanilla:launch` was rerun against `target/live-smoke/vanilla` and passed. The ignored launcher-core smoke installed/used the real vanilla artifacts and managed Java path as needed, started the actual Minecraft Java process, verified it survived startup, and stopped it through the process registry. This keeps the manual/custom vanilla profile launch foundation backed by current live evidence.

Latest profile launch-customization editor: Library profile rows now expose the launch customization fields already supported by the Rust profile model and launch planner: custom window width/height, default server name/address/port, and JVM args. Save validation covers complete/valid resolution values and valid server ports, sends `jvmArgs`, `resolution`, `defaultServer`, `clearResolution`, and `clearDefaultServer` through `update_profile`, and the web-preview fallback mutates or clears the same fields so the summary reflects the saved launch shape. Native launcher-core now supports clearing existing optional resolution/default-server values instead of only setting/replacing them, while rejecting contradictory set+clear requests. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "profile editor"`, `cargo test -p launcher-core update_profile -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "dense desktop panels|home screen fits"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Library profile Repair action: profile rows now expose `Repair` beside Play and Command, so manual/custom profiles can directly plan and run the native `repair_profile` flow from Library instead of waiting for a failed launch recovery. The action uses the shared repair lifecycle path, lands on Activity > Events with the repair plan/completion rows, refreshes the bootstrap snapshot afterward, and disables as `Running` while the profile has an active managed Minecraft process. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library profile repair|profile editor|delete action is disabled while a profile process is running"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "dense desktop panels|home screen fits"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest lifecycle launch-control alignment: Home pack Play, pack-card Play, Library profile Play, and friend quick-join controls now disable while install/repair/delete/import/runtime lifecycle work is active, matching the native Tauri lifecycle gate that already rejects launches during those operations. `launchProfile` and `joinFriend` also guard stale clicks with an activity message before invoking native commands. Pending repair Playwright coverage now verifies that Home repair keeps the active pack action disabled, Library launch controls disabled, and Home/Friends Join buttons marked `Busy` while the native `repair_profile` promise is in flight. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "pending native repair|library profile repair|delete action is disabled|friend join is disabled"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Library process controls: Library profile rows now derive their active managed process from `THEBOYSLAUNCHER_PROFILE_ID` metadata and expose `Stop` plus `Log` beside profile lifecycle controls while a Minecraft process is running or stop-requested. The controls call the same native `stop_managed_process` and `export_managed_process_log` paths used by Activity, keep Stop disabled as `Stopping` once a stop is requested, and preserve the existing running-profile guards for Play/Repair/Save/Archive/Delete. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads event log fallback|library delete action is disabled while a profile process is running"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Library profile Update action: Library profile rows now detect pack-backed profiles whose matching pack summary is `update_available` and expose an `Update` action beside Play/Repair/Command. The action uses the existing native `install_pack` update workflow, switches to disabled `Updating...` while the operation is in flight, refreshes the native bootstrap snapshot afterward, and disappears once the pack/profile reports the installed pack version is current. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "updating a pack plans native install workflow|pack update action is disabled while native install is pending|profile editor"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest live WinterPack happy-path recheck: on June 27, 2026, the real Dylan catalog plus WinterPack packwiz/Forge path was reverified end to end using the shared `target/live-smoke/winterpack` root. `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:metadata`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:plan`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:install`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:repair`, and `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch` all passed. This confirms the current remote catalog/pack.toml still parses, install planning still derives packwiz and Forge metadata, real artifact install reaches launch preflight, repair restores a deleted launch dependency, and the real WinterPack Java process survives startup and can be stopped through the launcher process registry.

Latest Library launch command preview: the Library `Command` action now renders a compact command preview panel instead of only replacing the sidebar status with an arg count. The panel shows whether the command is offline or stored-session authenticated, the profile name, Java executable, working directory, argument count, environment count, and the first launch arguments with bounded wrapping/scrolling for the 1100x720 desktop floor. Native command inspection still uses the authenticated `build_stored_authenticated_launch_command` route when a usable stored Minecraft session is present, and Playwright verifies the panel against a native-stubbed authenticated command. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "launch command action|profile editor|dense desktop panels"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity failed-step visibility: Activity Overview operation breakdowns now count failed launcher events separately instead of folding every `progressPercent: 100` event into completed work. Failed install/update/repair/launch operations now show a distinct failed-count pill in the latest-operation summary while still preserving completed planning steps and queued steps, making the failure state visible before switching into raw Events. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "failed native pack install|activity screen loads event log fallback|pack update action is disabled"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity process refresh control: Activity > Processes now has an explicit `Refresh` control beside `Clear exited` and `Live`, calling the existing native/web-preview managed-process load path on demand. This gives users a manual way to refresh supervised Minecraft process output/state when Live is off or after Stop/Log actions, while preserving the existing auto-refresh toggle. The running-profile Library guard test was updated for the current profile lifecycle surface where Play/Update/Repair all become disabled `Running` controls when an update-available profile owns a process. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads event log fallback|library delete action is disabled while a profile process is running|exited native process row can relaunch"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity event refresh control: Activity now exposes a separate `Refresh events` control beside the process tools, calling the existing native/web-preview launcher event load path without requiring users to toggle away from the current Activity view. This gives install/update/repair/launch progress a manual refresh affordance while a user watches the operation stream. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest lifecycle-blocked event label polish: completed Activity event `Play` actions now use the actual active lifecycle label while temporarily disabled, showing `Installing...`, `Repairing...`, `Deleting...`, `Importing...`, or `Installing Java...` instead of always saying `Installing...`. The Activity toolbar tests were tightened to target the exact `Events` tab now that `Refresh events` is also present. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home repair action shows pending native repair state|activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity raw event context: raw Activity event rows now show the same operation/subject context as Overview steps, for example `Install pack - winterpack`, `Download artifacts - winterpack`, or `Managed process - preview-process`. This makes real install/repair/launch failures and download progress easier to tie back to the affected pack/profile while watching the Events stream. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "failed native pack install|activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity process log export panel: Activity > Processes now keeps the most recent native process log export visible in a compact dismissible panel, showing the exported PID, retained line count, and filesystem path after the existing `export_managed_process_log` command succeeds. This gives users durable on-screen evidence of where the Minecraft stdout/stderr log was written instead of relying only on the transient sidebar status message. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "native process log export refreshes exited process state"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity stop-control label alignment: Activity > Processes now labels the stop control by process state: `Stop` while running, `Stopping` after a stop request, and `Stopped` for exited processes. This matches the Library process controls and makes supervised Minecraft shutdown state visible directly in the main process view. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "stopping a native process keeps playing presence while stop is pending"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest recovery Play lifecycle guard: sidebar recovery `Play` actions shown after Java-runtime or sign-in recovery now honor the same lifecycle lock as Home, Library, Friends, and Activity event-row launch controls. If install/repair/delete/import/runtime work is active, the recovery retry button is disabled and relabeled with the active lifecycle state such as `Installing...`, preventing stale retry clicks from racing native launcher work. The Java recovery Playwright path now holds a pack install open after Java recovery succeeds and verifies the recovery `Play` control is disabled as `Installing...` until the install completes. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library play action surfaces native Java runtime recovery and retry"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest launch-button lifecycle labels: Home pack-card and Library profile `Play` controls now explain lifecycle blocking instead of staying as plain `Play` while disabled. Installed pack cards use the active lifecycle label when unrelated install/repair/delete/import/runtime work blocks launching, while profile rows show `Busy` when their own adjacent Update/Repair/Delete action is already in flight and keep `Running` ahead of all lifecycle labels when a managed Minecraft process owns the profile. The pending-update Playwright regression now explicitly checks the duplicate running-control case while preserving the guarded update behavior. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "pack update action is disabled while native install is pending"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest process-log launch context: launcher-core process log exports now include a redacted `env:` section in addition to process ids, timing, arguments, and captured stdout/stderr. This preserves useful launch context such as `THEBOYSLAUNCHER_PROFILE_ID=winterpack` for support/debugging while still redacting token/secret/password/credential-like environment values before writing the exported Minecraft process log. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process_log_export_writes_summary_and_output_lines -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest process-log truncation visibility: native process log exports now return retained, total, and dropped output-line counts, and Activity > Processes displays that richer summary in both the sidebar status and the last-exported-log panel. Long Minecraft sessions now report e.g. `2/5 lines retained - 3 dropped` after export instead of implying the retained tail is the full process log. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process_log_export_writes_summary_and_output_lines -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "native process log export refreshes exited process state"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest launch event Java context: launcher-core launch start/failure operation events now include the Java executable used after launch planning, e.g. `Launch process started with pid 42 using java.` or `Launch process failed to start using missing-java: ...`. This gives Activity enough context to diagnose wrong-runtime or missing-executable launch failures without opening the command preview or exported logs. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core launch_ -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity operation row states: Activity Overview operation rows now receive explicit `failed`, `completed`, or `active` state classes based on the latest event and use subtle state styling, making failed install/repair/launch operations stand out before a user opens raw Events. The failed native pack install regression now checks that the failed operation row is visibly marked as failed while preserving retry behavior. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "failed native pack install|activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest pending artifact progress visibility: Activity artifact summaries now recognize `Artifact pending:` events in addition to downloading/downloaded/already-present/failed artifact events, and the Overview artifact strip shows a pending count before downloads begin. The web-preview fallback stream now includes a pending mod artifact so Playwright verifies `1 pending`, `2 started`, `2 finished`, and `0 failed` in the latest artifact progress summary. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest completed lifecycle Play fallback: Activity event rows for successful install/update/reinstall/repair events now derive a launch target from the completed event subject id even if the refreshed snapshot has not yet materialized a matching profile row. The UI still prefers the real profile name when available, but falls back to the pack name/subject id so the post-install `Play` action can launch immediately instead of disappearing due to a stale profile list. The native install Playwright regression now keeps `profiles` empty after install and verifies the completed event launches `winterpack` through `start_launch_process`. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "installing a pack refreshes"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest WinterPack delete smoke alias: `package.json` now exposes `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:delete`, matching the existing live-smoke runner support for the ignored `live_winterpack_delete_removes_profile_data_but_keeps_shared_cache` launcher-core test. This makes the install/launch/delete happy-path evidence easier to rerun from npm without remembering the raw script argument. Verified with `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:delete`, which installed/preflighted the real WinterPack artifacts under `target/live-smoke/winterpack`, deleted the managed `winterpack` profile directory, removed it from profiles, and confirmed shared cache/asset data remained.

Latest import-to-Library flow: successful profile imports now clear the active import plan, refresh native launcher state without overwriting the completion status message, and switch directly to the Library/Profiles view so the imported profile is visible and ready for Play/Repair/Command management. Web-preview import execution now mirrors that shape by adding a local imported profile from the import plan and landing on Profiles instead of leaving the user on the Import screen with only a mocked status message. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "import screen plans a preview migration"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest profile creator validation guard: the Library `New profile` form now reuses the existing create-profile validation to disable `Create` while the draft is incomplete or invalid, so blank names/versions or invalid memory values cannot fire a native `create_profile` command. The web-preview regression now proves a blank profile name disables Create, then re-enables it for a valid NeoForge profile and still adds the profile to Library. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library new profile action"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity relaunch lifecycle guard: exited-process `Play` controls in Activity > Processes now honor the same lifecycle lock as Home, Library, Friends, recovery, and event-row Play controls. While install/repair/delete/import/runtime work is active, the relaunch button is disabled and labeled with the active lifecycle state such as `Repairing...`, instead of appearing clickable and then only being rejected by `launchProfile`. The pending native repair regression now includes an exited WinterPack process and verifies its Activity relaunch control is disabled as `Repairing...`, then returns to an enabled `Play` once repair completes. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home repair action shows pending native repair state"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest desktop delete confirmation: Library profile deletion now uses an inline profile-row confirmation state instead of a browser `window.confirm` dialog. Clicking `Delete` reveals `Confirm delete` and `Cancel` in the row; confirming still calls the existing native `delete_profile` command and surfaces delete events in Activity, while canceling leaves the profile visible and reports `Profile deletion canceled`. The delete regressions were updated away from dialog handlers and now cover preview removal, cancel, native success event streaming, native failure preservation, and running-process guards. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library delete action"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Settings Java lifecycle label alignment: the Settings managed-Java `Install runtime` button now uses the same lifecycle-blocking label as the rest of the launcher. While install/repair/delete/import/runtime work is already active, the disabled button shows the active state such as `Repairing...` instead of a generic install label that looks stale-clickable. The pending native repair regression now verifies the Settings runtime install control is disabled as `Repairing...` alongside Home, Library, Friends, and Activity relaunch guards. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home repair action shows pending native repair state"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity process cleanup state: Activity > Processes now disables `Clear exited` whenever the managed-process list has no exited rows to clear. This keeps the process supervisor from presenting a no-op cleanup action after exited/crashed rows have already been removed while leaving stop-requested/running rows visible. The Activity fallback regression now verifies `Clear exited` is enabled while the preview crashed process is present and disabled after the cleanup removes it. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Microsoft auth setup visibility: native Sign in now surfaces `start_microsoft_auth_flow` setup errors directly instead of falling through to the older placeholder `start_microsoft_login` receipt. This makes missing desktop auth configuration such as `THEBOYS_MICROSOFT_CLIENT_ID is required` visible in the launcher status card while preserving the web-preview mocked sign-in fallback. If the local browser callback listener fails or times out, the app now moves to Settings so the Microsoft callback URL field and `Complete sign in` action are immediately visible. Manual pasted-callback completion now also uses the native error helper, so token-exchange failures such as `Microsoft token exchange failed: invalid_grant` show as actionable sign-in errors instead of pretending completion is mocked. The sign-in regressions verify the native setup error is shown, `start_microsoft_login` is not invoked after auth-start failure, callback guard behavior remains intact, local callback failure lands on Settings, and pasted-callback token failures do not continue into `authenticate_and_save_minecraft_session`. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "sign in action|callback completion|callback completion guard"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest friend quick-join snapshot refresh: successful native friend/server joins now refresh the bootstrap snapshot after the managed Minecraft process starts, matching the normal Library/Home Play path. This keeps Library profile state such as durable `lastPlayed` markers current after a quick-join launch instead of only after direct Play. The authenticated friend-join regression now stubs the post-launch snapshot with `lastPlayed: unix:1710000200` and verifies the Library profile row shows `Last played` after joining. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "stored session friend join passes server target"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest refreshable-session authenticated launch alignment: renderer-safe stored Minecraft sessions now keep the non-secret Microsoft client id while still redacting the Minecraft access token, Microsoft refresh token, Microsoft user id, and scopes. The React shell treats sessions with a client id as native-refreshable even if their displayed Minecraft token is expired, so Play, friend Join, command inspection, backend session exchange, and presence publishing use the stored-auth native commands and let Tauri refresh before launch instead of silently falling back to offline launch. Settings now labels those expired Microsoft-backed sessions as `refreshes on launch` so the visible status matches the authenticated launch behavior. Launcher-core refresh metadata merging now preserves existing Microsoft refresh/user/scope metadata when a refresh-token response omits optional fields, and `refresh_saved_minecraft_session` reattaches refresh metadata after Minecraft re-authentication. The command-preview regression now also uses an expired refreshable renderer-safe session and verifies `build_stored_authenticated_launch_command` is selected over the offline command path. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "launch command action shows native command preview|expired refreshable stored session"`, `cargo test -p launcher-core stored_session_ -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher renderer_safe_session -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest live WinterPack catalog recheck: on June 27, 2026, the live Dylan catalog and WinterPack standard `pack.toml` path still resolved successfully. `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:metadata` passed `live_dylan_catalog_and_winterpack_packwiz_metadata_parse`, and `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:plan` passed `live_remote_winterpack_install_planning_uses_packwiz_and_forge_metadata`, confirming the current remote catalog/packwiz metadata still derives the expected WinterPack/Forge install plan.

Latest Microsoft desktop auth live check: the real Microsoft/Minecraft sign-in flow was exercised with client id `d10dfc60-1a42-44a8-b3af-edf4f5ee2c1f` against an isolated `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR=target/interactive-auth-smoke`. The launcher now opens the Microsoft auth URL through a narrow native `open_microsoft_auth_url` command instead of relying on `window.open` inside WebView2; the command only accepts `https://login.live.com/oauth20_authorize.srf` URLs. Windows uses `rundll32.exe url.dll,FileProtocolHandler` so the system browser opens correctly. The OAuth redirect was aligned with Microsoft's Mobile/Desktop app registration behavior: register `http://localhost` in Azure, while the launcher requests and listens for `http://localhost:53682/`; localhost port matching is handled by Microsoft, and the local one-shot listener accepts the root `/?code=...&state=...` callback. A real browser callback completed successfully and persisted a stored Minecraft session for `Dilll` under `target/interactive-auth-smoke/config/minecraft-session.json` with refresh metadata. A live WinterPack launch smoke against the same isolated root passed after sign-in. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core microsoft_`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher microsoft_`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch -- --root target\interactive-auth-smoke`.

Latest live WinterPack repair overwrite fix: a fresh `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:repair` initially exposed a real Windows repair/install failure when a non-preserved pack file already existed but no longer matched the expected hash: the direct pack-file downloader wrote a sibling `.download` file and then tried to `fs::rename` over the existing destination, which can fail on Windows with `The system cannot find the file specified`/replacement behavior around stale destinations. Download writes now use the existing Windows-aware `replace_file_with_temporary` helper and include destination/temp path context on directory, write, and replacement errors. A new `download_item_writer_replaces_existing_stale_pack_file` regression covers replacing a stale WinterPack-style config file. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core download_item_writer -- --test-threads=1`, rerun `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:repair` (now passes), `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch` (passed before this fix in the same continuation), `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest live WinterPack profile lifecycle recheck: after the repair overwrite fix, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:delete` passed against `target/live-smoke/winterpack`, confirming managed WinterPack profile data is removed while shared cache/artifact data remains. A follow-up `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:install` also passed, proving the launcher can recreate the supported managed pack profile and reach launch preflight cleanly after deletion. A final `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch` after that delete/reinstall cycle passed too, confirming the recreated profile can start a real Minecraft process and stop it cleanly. `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace` were green before the final post-reinstall launch smoke.

Latest desktop launcher status strip: the React shell now has a compact `Launcher quick status` strip below the topbar so the 1100x720+ desktop UI always exposes session state, active process/log access, and latest launcher operation without requiring users to hunt through the denser panels. The strip routes Session to Settings, Processes to Activity/Processes with a refresh, and Latest to Activity/Overview with event refresh. Playwright coverage now asserts the default strip labels, Settings routing, and that a native launch mock updates the strip to `1 active process` while the process supervisor/log controls remain visible. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen renders|library play action opens native process supervisor"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "home screen fits|dense desktop panels fit|launcher status card fits"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest process log reveal workflow: exported managed-process logs now include an `Open logs` action in the Activity/Processes export receipt. The React shell invokes the new Tauri `reveal_exported_process_log` command and reports either the native open result or the web-preview fallback. The Tauri command validates that the requested log path resolves under the launcher log directory before asking the OS file manager to reveal it, so the UI gets a real post-export next step without adding broad arbitrary file-open behavior. Coverage includes `cargo test -p theboyslauncher exported_process_log_path_validation -- --test-threads=1` for allowed/rejected paths and `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "native process log export refreshes exited process state"` for the Activity banner action. `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace` are green after this change.

Latest launch failure recovery navigation: native Play and friend Join failures now move the shell to Activity/Overview after refreshing launcher events, so users land on the failed operation, latest-step detail, and repair/sign-in/Java recovery actions instead of staying on Library/Friends with only the sidebar status message. Web-preview fallback behavior is unchanged. Playwright now asserts both library launch preflight failures and friend-join preflight failures automatically land on Activity/Overview before using the existing repair recovery buttons. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library play action surfaces native launch preflight errors|friend join surfaces native launch preflight repair recovery"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest install/update customization preservation: native pack install/update/reinstall now mirrors repair by preserving an existing managed profile's user launch customizations when it fetches the fresh catalog/packwiz profile. The live catalog still supplies updated pack metadata such as `installedPackVersion`, loader, and game version, but saved memory, JVM args, custom resolution, default server, and `lastPlayed` survive the install/update persistence step instead of being reset to catalog defaults. A focused Tauri regression covers the merge behavior, and the Library pack-update Playwright test now verifies that after the native update refresh the visible profile still shows custom `8 GB RAM`, `1600x900`, `The Custom Cabin`, and `2 JVM args` while the Update action disappears. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher install_profile_merge -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "updating a pack plans native install workflow before refreshing status"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Java executable launch preflight: launcher-core command-spec validation now rejects explicit missing Java executable paths, such as a deleted managed-runtime `bin/java.exe`, before process spawn and uses the managed-Java recovery wording (`Install a managed Java runtime from Settings before launching`). Bare executable names such as `java` or `missing-java` still pass command-spec construction so PATH/spawn behavior remains available for system Java. Focused core regressions cover both cases alongside the existing launch artifact preflight suite, and Playwright now verifies the exact missing-managed-Java executable message lands on Activity/Overview, exposes the sidebar and event-row `Java` recovery actions, and opens Settings with the Temurin recommendation selected instead of offering Repair. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core process_command_spec_ -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "Java runtime recovery|missing managed Java executable"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest repair planning decoupled from Java selection: launcher-core `plan_repair_profile` now validates the saved profile directly and builds repair-operation progress from profile metadata instead of constructing a full launch plan. This keeps launch preflight strict, but lets Repair planning remain available for custom/vanilla profiles even when Java discovery is currently broken or a managed Java runtime needs to be reinstalled. The actual repair execution path still performs its required downloads and processor work. Verified with `cargo test -p launcher-core repair_plan -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core repair_profile_rejects_unknown_profiles -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p launcher-core native_actions_validate_required_subjects -- --test-threads=1`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:repair`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest native lifecycle failure text alignment: Tauri `install_pack`, `repair_profile`, and `delete_profile` now return the same prefixed failure messages they record to Activity (`Pack install failed: ...`, `Profile repair failed: ...`, and `Profile delete failed: ...`) instead of returning only the raw inner error after recording a richer event. This keeps the sidebar/status surface, Activity history, and Playwright shell expectations aligned for real native failures. A focused formatter regression locks the shared wording, and the delete failure E2E still verifies the profile remains visible with the Activity error surfaced. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher native_operation_failure_message -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher failed_native_operation_event -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library delete action keeps profile visible after native failure"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest Activity process supervisor cleanup: exited process rows no longer render a disabled stop control. They now offer the useful lifecycle actions only, such as `Play` when the profile can be relaunched and `Log` for output export, while running and stop-requested rows keep their stop/stopping controls. The affected Playwright selectors were also scoped to the Activity view tabs so the launcher quick-status `Processes` button does not create strict-mode ambiguity. A live WinterPack plan smoke was rerun after this UI slice and still resolved the current remote Dylan catalog plus packwiz/Forge metadata. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "exited native process row can relaunch its profile from Activity"`, `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:plan`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest install/repair Activity grouping: native install and repair completion events now reuse the operation id from the plan that was already recorded instead of creating a separate one-event operation for the final success message. Post-plan install/repair failures now carry the same plan id too, so Activity can show the queued/planned work and final failure together instead of splitting the actionable error into a separate operation row. The React Activity overview now also sorts operation summaries by each operation's latest event update, not by the first time the operation id appeared, so a completed or failed install/repair can rise back to the top after intervening artifact download operations. This keeps Activity overview/detail grouped around the actual install or repair workflow so users see plan, progress, and completion/failure as one launcher operation. Standalone native lifecycle events such as delete still create their own operation id. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher completed_planned_native_operation_event -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher completed_native_operation_event -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher failed_planned_native_operation_event -- --test-threads=1`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "activity overview sorts operations by latest event update|activity screen loads event log fallback"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest stop-process Activity refresh: after a successful native `stop_managed_process`, the React shell now refreshes launcher events immediately before updating the status message. This makes the core managed-process lifecycle event, such as `Stop requested for pid ...`, appear in Activity/Overview without requiring a manual `Refresh events` click while preserving the existing process-row stop-requested state and presence behavior. The stop-pending Playwright regression now stubs the managed-process event stream and verifies the stop event appears in Activity before returning to the Processes view. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "stopping a native process keeps playing presence while stop is pending"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest process cleanup cache hygiene: `clear_exited_managed_processes` now also prunes the renderer's short-lived recent-launch fallback cache for any process id not returned by native cleanup. This prevents a just-cleared exited process from reappearing on the next process refresh if the registry refresh had previously lagged behind the launch return value. The log export regression was also aligned with the current process supervisor behavior: exited rows no longer show a disabled Stop control, but still keep Log available. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library play action keeps returned native process visible when process refresh lags|clear exited process does not restore recent launch fallback|native process log export refreshes exited process state"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest managed Java install event sync: after a successful managed-Java runtime download/install sequence, the React shell now refreshes the native launcher event log before showing the final install status. This keeps Activity aligned with the Tauri-recorded download/install operation history instead of relying only on locally merged returned operation plans, which matters for Java recovery after launch preflight failures. The native managed-Java Playwright regression now verifies `list_launcher_events` runs after `execute_managed_java_runtime_install`. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "settings managed Java install runs native download and install commands"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest live WinterPack launch recheck: `npm run smoke:live:winterpack:launch` passed against `target/live-smoke/winterpack`, exercising the current live WinterPack install artifacts, launch preflight, real Java process startup, process-registry supervision, and clean stop path. This is the current strongest evidence that the launcher-core happy path can still install enough real pack/Forge/vanilla artifacts to start Minecraft after the recent UI/event-log work. Verified after the smoke with `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest native launch event sync: successful native Play and friend Join launches now refresh the launcher event log immediately after the managed Minecraft process is returned and before the shell lands on Activity/Processes. This makes launch-profile operation history visible even if the Tauri event listener misses the earliest start event, while preserving the process supervisor as the primary post-launch view. The native launch Playwright regression now stubs the launch event, verifies it appears in Activity/Overview, and asserts `list_launcher_events` runs after `start_launch_process`; related friend-join selectors were scoped to Activity tabs to avoid ambiguity with the desktop quick-status `Processes` shortcut. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library play action opens native process supervisor after launch"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "friend join|authenticated join|joining a friend"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest launch command diagnostics: the Library command preview now includes a `Copy command` action that writes the full renderer-safe launch command, including working directory and environment lines, instead of only exposing the first visible argument chips. It tries the browser/WebView clipboard API first and falls back to a temporary textarea copy path when clipboard permissions are unavailable. This helps debug real Forge/Fabric/Java launch problems while preserving the existing native redaction boundary for access tokens. The command-preview Playwright regression exercises the fallback copy path, verifies the copied command includes overflow arguments and the profile environment marker, and confirms the access token remains redacted. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts --grep "library launch command action shows native command preview"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, and `cargo check --workspace`.

Latest process output diagnostics: Activity > Processes now includes `Copy output` beside `Log`, copying the currently retained output tail with stream prefixes through the same clipboard/textarea fallback used by command diagnostics. This gives users a quick way to share the visible Minecraft stdout/stderr tail without exporting a file, while the full retained log export remains available.
