Latest product-flow update: the create-profile and profile-customization UIs now keep the full Minecraft manifest instead of filtering to releases only, add friendly version-type dropdowns for `Releases`, `Snapshots`, `Old beta`, and `Old alpha`, and then let users choose a valid version from that category rather than typing a raw version string. The default create flow still opens on the newest release, while snapshot/historical testing profiles can be created and edited from dropdowns. The Library view now quietly loads the version manifest so existing profile customization has real version options without first opening the new-profile form. A rendered in-app-browser pass found and fixed a web-preview fallback edge where selecting `Snapshots` with no snapshot manifest data incorrectly displayed release fallback versions; non-release categories now show an empty disabled version selector until real manifest data exists. Verified with `npm run build`, `cargo check --workspace`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "creating a profile refreshes the native bootstrap snapshot"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "profile.*version|creating a profile refreshes"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "library profile editor saves preview changes|library profile editor can choose snapshot versions from the manifest"`, and in-app browser QA at `http://127.0.0.1:1420/`.

Latest product-flow update 2: Play and Join launches that fail native preflight because launch artifacts are missing now automatically run the existing repair planner/executor once, then retry the original action. This means first Play on a fresh vanilla/custom profile should install the missing launch files and continue instead of dumping a new user into a repair error. If the one-shot repair retry still fails, the recovery UI remains available: failed Play launches offer `Repair and play`, while failed friend/server joins remember the attempted server target in the renderer and offer `Repair and join`, retrying `start_launch_process_for_server` with the same server payload instead of falling back to plain Play. Launch Details preflight failures intentionally keep the calmer `Repair` action because they were not user-initiated Play attempts. Activity failed-launch rows mirror the current recovery intent when it is still known, while pack/profile repair-needed states remain plain repair controls. The vanilla compatibility sweep has been delegated to background agent Pauli so the main thread can focus on launch-flow and product work without overlapping the version matrix. Verified with `npm run build` and `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "library play action surfaces native launch preflight errors|friend join surfaces native launch preflight repair recovery|launch command preflight failure offers repair recovery"`.

Latest product-flow update 3: native profile imports now participate in the renderer lifecycle busy guard. `executeImportPlan` refuses duplicate submissions while an import is pending, sets the lifecycle key for the profile import, disables the Import button, shows `Importing...`, and clears the busy state when the native operation finishes or fails. This closes a real desktop-flow issue where a slow import could be double-clicked and submitted twice before the imported profile appears in Library. Verified with `npm run build` and `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "import screen plans a preview migration|native import disables duplicate submissions while pending"`.

Latest packaged-runtime check: release packaging artifacts are present and verify successfully. The packaged release executable `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe` was smoke-started from the built output, appeared as a `theboyslauncher` process, and was stopped cleanly; it did not leave a launcher/backend process behind. A follow-up Windows Computer Use pass launched the same release exe as a user-facing desktop app, captured the real `TheBoysLauncher` window at roughly 1280x852, and verified the packaged UI rendered the Clean Dark shell with `Desktop connected`, signed-in account `Dilll`, WinterPack ready-to-play state, Play/Repair controls, and the main sidebar. Sidebar navigation through Library, Activity, Settings, Import, and back to Play/Home worked in the packaged app; this pass clicked only the sidebar `Play` navigation, not a Minecraft launch button. The packaged window was then closed with Alt+F4 through Computer Use, and `list_apps` reported no remaining TheBoysLauncher/social-backend app. Existing release bundles verified with `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, covering `target/release/bundle/msi/TheBoysLauncher_4.0.0_x64_en-US.msi` and `target/release/bundle/nsis/TheBoysLauncher_4.0.0_x64-setup.exe`. Release resources verified with `npm run verify:package-resources`, confirming `src-tauri/resources/social-backend.exe` and portable `src-tauri/resources/social-backend` match the release `target/release/social-backend.exe`. A stale debug `target/debug/social-backend.exe` process from June 27 was stopped because it locked the debug backend build; after that, `npm run build:social-backend:debug`, `npm run stage:social-backend:debug`, and `npm run verify:package-resources:debug` passed. Release resources were restored afterward with `npm run stage:social-backend` and reverified with `npm run verify:package-resources`. Security config also passed with `npm run verify:tauri-security`.

Latest packaged-runtime fix: the Computer Use packaged-exe pass revealed a real process-level cleanup bug: closing `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe` stopped the window but left a managed `target/release/social-backend.exe` child running. `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` now routes `SocialBackendService::stop()` through a synchronous `stop_managed_child()` helper and registers a Tauri `on_window_event` hook that stops the managed backend child when the window is destroyed. Added backend unit coverage with a throwaway long-running child process to prove `stop_managed_child()` clears and kills the managed child. After rebuilding with `npm run tauri:build`, another Windows Computer Use pass launched the rebuilt release exe, verified the packaged UI text (`Desktop connected`, `Dilll signed in`, WinterPack ready state, sidebar navigation), closed it with Alt+F4, and both Computer Use `list_apps` plus PowerShell `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend` showed no remaining launcher/backend process. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher backend_`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run tauri:build`, `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, and `npm run verify:package-resources`.

Latest packaged-runtime fix 2: the next packaged-exe pass caught the Windows backend console window that appeared when the packaged launcher started its bundled `social-backend.exe`. `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` now applies the Windows `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` process creation flag to the managed backend child while still piping stdin/stdout/stderr to null. The release app was rebuilt with `npm run tauri:build`, launched through Windows Computer Use from `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe`, allowed to reach the signed-in Home shell, then navigated to Settings where the backend was reachable; Computer Use `list_apps`/`list_windows` showed only the `TheBoysLauncher` window and no backend console/terminal window. Closing the packaged app with Alt+F4 removed it from Computer Use, and `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend` showed no remaining launcher/backend processes. Verified with `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, `cargo test -p theboyslauncher backend_`, and `npm run tauri:build`.

Latest UI cleanup update: a dedicated UI/app-flow subagent audited the current Clean Dark Glassless implementation at 1366x768 and found the remaining high-value clutter concentrated in stale Playwright expectations, Profile Customize, Settings Advanced, Activity log density, and missing/unfinished modal/menu surfaces. The main thread fixed the verified quick wins: Home/Friends/Library shell assertions now match the current seeded preview data and headings, Profile Customize keeps only name/version/loader/memory visible by default and hides window size, default server, JVM args, and Java override under Advanced, and Pack Details now has a small real More menu with currently working actions instead of placeholder-only items. Verified with `npm run build` and `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "home screen renders|home details action|library profile editor saves preview changes|dense desktop panels|friends screen starts"`; 10 focused tests passed across the normal desktop and minimum desktop Playwright projects.

Latest UI cleanup update 2: Activity and Settings were tightened further for the Clean Dark desktop direction. The Activity overview now keeps latest-operation step/event details collapsed behind `View details` by default, while leaving summary progress, counts, and file-progress chips visible; this keeps artifact/download noise out of the first glance without hiding diagnostics. Settings Advanced now has a friendly header and keeps its diagnostics readout inside the advanced panel as a grouped `Current launcher state` card, so normal Settings stays focused and Java/backend tools remain hidden until Advanced is opened. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "activity screen loads event log fallback|activity overview sorts operations"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "settings render memory and offline profile data"`, and `npm run tauri:build`. The rebuilt `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe` was launched through Windows Computer Use, rendered the signed-in Clean Dark Home shell with WinterPack and `Desktop connected`, closed with Alt+F4, and both Computer Use plus `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend` showed no remaining launcher/backend process.

Latest UI cleanup update 3: multiple Minecraft accounts now have a dedicated Clean Dark account manager modal instead of living as a long embedded list on the normal Settings screen. The signed-in topbar account button opens `Manage accounts`, Settings shows a compact saved-account summary plus `Manage accounts`, and the modal owns add/finish sign-in, refresh, switch active account, and sign-out/remove confirmation actions while reusing the existing native account commands. The normal Settings account card is less cluttered for new-to-PC users, while the multiple-account workflow remains obvious and test-covered. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "settings lists stored Minecraft accounts|settings native account actions|settings native mode keeps renderer-created preview session actions hidden|settings can remove a stored Minecraft account"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "settings lists stored Minecraft accounts"`, and `npm run tauri:build`. The rebuilt packaged exe was launched through Windows Computer Use; clicking the signed-in topbar account opened the `Manage accounts` dialog with `Dilll`, `Add another account`, and `Refresh accounts`, then closing the app left no `theboyslauncher` or `social-backend` process by both Computer Use and `Get-Process`.

Latest UI cleanup update 4: the import review step now uses a focused Clean Dark modal instead of rendering as another dense block at the bottom of the Import page. The Import page remains a simple scan/review/import guide with candidates, while clicking `Review` opens a `Review profile import` dialog with source-safe copy, profile metadata, ready-size summary, conflict decisions, cancel, and the existing guarded Import action. The native import execution path and duplicate-submit guard are unchanged. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "import screen exposes scan action|import screen plans a preview migration|native import disables duplicate submissions"`, and `npm run tauri:build`. The rebuilt packaged exe was launched through Windows Computer Use, navigated to Import, and rendered the clean scan/review/import page; closing the app left no `theboyslauncher` or `social-backend` process by both Computer Use and `Get-Process`.

Latest product-flow update 4: native profile deletion now explains the shared-cache behavior that previously made reinstalls look suspicious. `delete_profile` still removes only the managed profile directory and catalog entry, preserving shared Minecraft downloads/assets for faster future installs, but its success receipt and launcher event now say: `Profile files were removed; shared Minecraft downloads were kept for faster reinstalls.` This should make later `File already present` artifact messages understandable after deleting and reinstalling a profile. Verified with `cargo test -p theboyslauncher delete_profile_success_message_explains_shared_cache_retention`, `cargo check -p theboyslauncher`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "library delete action clears stale launch recovery"`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "library delete action surfaces native event stream after removal"`, and `npm run tauri:build`. The rebuilt packaged exe was launched through Windows Computer Use, rendered the signed-in Clean Dark Home shell, closed with Alt+F4, and both Computer Use plus `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend` showed no remaining launcher/backend process.

Latest product-flow update 5: the sidebar live-progress chip now treats child download/processor subjects such as `winterpack-modloader-artifacts`, `winterpack-direct-pack-files`, `winterpack-metafile-downloads`, `winterpack-auxiliary`, and `winterpack-dependencies` as part of the parent profile lifecycle when deciding whether a download is still active. This fixes the real desktop symptom where the bottom-left status could stay on `Downloading files` with a final processor message after the parent install/repair had already completed. The renderer also recognizes the newer shared-cache delete completion text when clearing delete busy state. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "live file download events surface in the sidebar status card"`, `npm run build`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

Latest packaged-runtime Play proof: after the progress fix, `npm run tauri:build` rebuilt the release app and both bundle verifiers still passed. The rebuilt `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe` was launched through Windows Computer Use, rendered the signed-in Clean Dark shell with `Dilll`, `Desktop connected`, and WinterPack ready, then the real WinterPack `Play` button was clicked in the packaged UI. The launcher moved through `Launching authenticated profile`, disabled duplicate Play/Repair actions, started a real `Minecraft* 1.20.1` Java window from `C:/Program Files/Microsoft/jdk-17.0.19.10-hotspot/bin/java.exe`, switched to Activity, and showed WinterPack as `RUNNING` with `1 active process`. The process was then stopped through the launcher's own Activity `Stop` control; the Java/Minecraft window disappeared, Activity showed WinterPack `STOPPED`, the relaunch Play action returned, and both Computer Use plus `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend,java,javaw` showed no remaining launcher/backend/Java process after closing the packaged app. Verified with `npm run tauri:build`, `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, Windows Computer Use packaged Play/Stop/close, and the final process check.

Latest packaged-runtime install-to-play proof: the packaged exe was launched with `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_ROOT_DIR=target/packaged-ui-install-smoke` so the test used a fresh isolated data/config/cache/log root. The first pass proved the native install was actually downloading files into the isolated cache, but also exposed that Activity and the bottom-left status only updated after manually pressing `Refresh`. The renderer now polls `list_launcher_events` while install/repair/delete/import/Java/launch lifecycle work is pending and reconciles completed lifecycle events returned from polling, so long native work updates without user refresh. Playwright covers the regression with `pending native install polls launcher events into the sidebar without refresh`. After rebuilding with `npm run tauri:build`, the isolated packaged UI was relaunched from a clean root, WinterPack `Install` was clicked, and without pressing Refresh the sidebar changed to `Downloading files` with current file/progress while Activity populated live download rows. The install completed successfully, persisted `config/profiles.json` plus `data/profiles/winterpack`, returned Home to `Ready` with Play/Repair, then the real packaged Play button launched `Minecraft* 1.20.1`; Activity showed WinterPack `RUNNING`, Stop terminated Java, Activity labeled the row `STOPPED`, and closing the packaged app left no `theboyslauncher`, `social-backend`, `java`, or `javaw` process. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "pending native install polls launcher events into the sidebar without refresh"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run tauri:build`, `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, Windows Computer Use install/progress/play/stop/close, and final process checks.

Latest product-flow update 6: Play launches that fail because the selected Minecraft version needs a newer Java runtime now prepare managed Java automatically instead of sending the user to Settings. The renderer parses the required major Java version from the native preflight failure, loads the recommended runtime manifest, downloads/installs the best matching managed runtime through the existing native Java install commands, clears Java recovery state, and retries the original Play action once. If the automatic Java install cannot complete, the previous Java recovery affordance remains available as a fallback. Playwright now covers the one-click Java recovery path: the first `start_launch_process` throws a Java 21 requirement, the renderer installs Temurin 21, and the second launch starts the process without invoking profile repair or pack install. The release exe was rebuilt with `npm run tauri:build`, verified with `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, launched through Windows Computer Use from `target/release/theboyslauncher.exe`, and the existing 1.21.8 vanilla profile that previously failed on Java 17 launched successfully into the real `Minecraft 1.21.8` main menu using `C:/Users/server/AppData/Roaming/theboys/TheBoysLauncher/data/runtimes/temurin-21-windows-x64/jdk-21.0.11+10/bin/java.exe`. The process was stopped through the launcher's Activity `Stop` action, Activity returned to `No active process`, closing the packaged app removed the managed backend, and final `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend,java,javaw` showed no leftovers. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "library play action surfaces native Java runtime recovery and retry"`, `npm run build`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `npm run tauri:build`, `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, Windows Computer Use packaged 1.21.8 Play/Stop/close, and final process checks.

Latest UI cleanup update 5: launcher recovery affordances no longer expose file repair language as the primary next action. The earlier UI sweep removed visible `Repair` buttons from primary Home/Library/Profile flows, changed repair-needed packs to present as normal `Play`, moved manual file verification under pack details `More > Check files`, and translated native `repair_profile` events into friendlier `Check files`/`Files are ready` copy. The main thread then softened failed-launch recovery buttons further: sidebar and Activity failed-launch actions now read `Try again`, `Try play again`, or `Try join again` while still running the existing background file check/retry path. This keeps recovery understandable for new-to-PC users while preserving the support escape hatch for corrupted installs. Verified with `npm run build`, `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "successful pack update clears stale launch repair recovery|launch command preflight failure offers file-check recovery|successful launch command preview clears stale repair recovery|library delete action clears stale launch recovery"`, and `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "launch command preflight failure offers file-check recovery|failed native repair surfaces the failed launcher event|friend join surfaces native launch preflight repair recovery"`.

Latest UI cleanup update 6: the packaged 1.21.8 smoke exposed a real Activity layout problem where the long managed Java path could create horizontal scrolling and push the process `Stop` action offscreen. Activity process rows now use a fixed grid for icon/details/actions, clip long executable paths inside the details column, and wrap the process actions beneath details at the minimum desktop width instead of widening the workspace. Added a regression with a long managed Temurin 21 executable path to prove the workspace and document do not horizontally overflow and the `Stop` action remains visible. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "dense desktop panels fit|activity process actions stay visible"`, `npm run build`, `npm run tauri:build`, `npm run verify:tauri-bundles`, and a final `Get-Process theboyslauncher,social-backend,java,javaw` check showing no open launcher/backend/Java processes.

Latest UI cleanup update 7: Activity process rows now use the friendly managed-process display name for action/output accessibility labels instead of raw executable names like `javaw.exe`. A running WinterPack row now exposes `WinterPack process actions` and `WinterPack output`, while unmapped process rows still fall back to `Minecraft`. This matches the visible row title and keeps screen-reader/test affordances aligned with the user-facing launcher model rather than Java internals. Verified with `npx playwright test tests/e2e/launcher-shell.spec.ts -g "home repair action shows pending native repair state|activity screen loads event log fallback|exited native process row can relaunch its profile from Activity|activity process actions stay visible"` and `npm run build`.

Latest vanilla snapshot continuation 41: the forty-second snapshot chunk passed all 5 selected versions at `--offset 205`: `23w06a`, `23w05a`, `23w04a`, `23w03a`, and `1.19.3-rc3`. Latest vanilla compatibility runner speedup: `scripts/run-vanilla-compat-campaign.mjs` now supports `--jobs <n>` for parallel isolated per-version roots, `--shared-cache` for a serial shared launcher cache rooted at `target/live-smoke/vanilla-compat-shared-cache`, and `--quiet` to capture cargo output while printing concise progress plus failure tails. The shared-cache path is intentionally serial-only for now because the downloader does not yet have file-level cache locks; the script rejects `--shared-cache --jobs 2` rather than risking concurrent writes. `LiveSmokeRoot` now honors `THEBOYS_LAUNCHER_LIVE_TEST_CACHE`, so live compatibility roots can keep data/config/log isolation while reusing common cache assets/libraries/natives/versions. Verified with `node --check scripts/run-vanilla-compat-campaign.mjs`, dry runs for `--jobs 2`, `--shared-cache`, and `--quiet`, the expected rejection for `--shared-cache --jobs 2`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p launcher-core`, and a real shared-cache smoke `npm run smoke:live:vanilla:compat -- --version 21w05a --shared-cache`, which passed and left only the intended shared cache while cleaning the per-version root and leaving no launcher/backend/Java processes.
